| E-2026-07-25-04 | 2026-07-25 | ERA (European Union Agency for Railways) — "ESC/RSC technical document", Doc ID TD/011REC1028, V43.0, 08/07/2026 (living register; first published v1.0 30/01/2020, ~monthly updates). Primary agency register listing the "ESC/RSC types" submitted by Infrastructure Managers per CCS TSI Annex I §4.2.17. Legal basis cited in-doc: Commission Implementing Reg (EU) 2023/1695 (CCS TSI, repealing 2016/919). Paraphrased: ESC = ETCS System Compatibility, RSC = Radio System Compatibility (voice + data) — the demonstrations that a vehicle's onboard ETCS / radio (GSM-R) is technically compatible with a given trackside ETCS system-type / radio-network on specific lines. Mechanism: IMs submit the compatibility-check definition to ERA (esc-rsc@era.europa.eu); ERA assigns a unique identifier (format ESC/RSC-<MS-or-EU>-<NN>-<V voice / D data>), publishes it here, and IMs classify lines by type in RINF while ERATV records the types checked per authorised vehicle type; VA (vehicle authorisation) applicants use ESC/RSC Statements. Type statuses: Valid / Reserved / Cancelled / For-information. Baseline ESC-EU-0 / RSC-EU-0 = "no check needed; subsystem certificates sufficient" (e.g. Greece, Ireland, Bulgaria; Germany's RSC-DE-01-V removed at v40 → DE folded under RSC-EU-0). Tables: ESC types (ch5), RSC-Voice (ch6), RSC-Data (ch7), Appendix A cancelled. Covers ~all EU MS + IMs (ÖBB, Infrabel, SBB, SŽDC, DB Netz, SNCF Réseau/OC'VIA/LISEA/EIFFAGE, RFI, ProRail, PKP, ADIF, Banedanmark, Network Rail/RSSB, …); many entries are BORDER-SECTION transition checks ("only the execution of the transition is part of the ESC check") | Agency primary technical document (EU Agency for Railways; versioned register under the CCS TSI) | ERA — ESC/RSC technical document TD/011REC1028 V43.0 (era.europa.eu, ERTMS section); file "esc-rsc_technical_document_en.pdf", Download folder | A | R7 (+ R6 compatibility-without-re-authorisation; R2 per-line radio-migration mechanics) | validate | A — PRIMARY: an official EU-Agency-for-Railways versioned, document-controlled register (TD/011REC1028 V43.0), the authoritative list of ESC/RSC compatibility types under the CCS TSI — same class as the ERA/MORANE-2 A-tier sources on file (E-2026-07-02-15, E-2026-07-19-01). CAVEATS: (1) LIVING REGISTER — v43 in ~6.5 yr (near-monthly), a SNAPSHOT whose individual type values change constantly; treat the FRAMEWORK/mechanism as load-bearing, not any frozen type value (verify current version for specifics); (2) ETCS-centric + GSM-R radio — RSC today = GSM-R voice/data; FRMCS is NOT yet a distinct RSC radio system in the register → FORWARD HOOK: FRMCS radio compatibility will need its OWN RSC types/framework as it deploys, and this GSM-R-era register is the machinery it must EXTEND into (not replace). VALIDATES R7 (interoperability + CCS-TSI conformance, Accepted · ADR-001) at the MECHANISM level: ESC/RSC is the concrete operational backbone by which the CCS TSI (Reg (EU) 2023/1695, cited in-doc) demonstrates vehicle↔route technical compatibility across borders — what "conform to CCS TSI" means in practice, complementing the SIM/roaming layer of the DB roaming list (E-2026-07-25-03). STRONG BEARING on R6 (don't re-qualify ETCS on every transport change, Accepted · ADR-001): ESC/RSC + ESC/RSC Statements are precisely the framework that manages route/radio compatibility via DEFINED CHECKS rather than full re-authorisation — the many border-section "transition" ESC types (SNCF Réseau DE/BE/CH/LU/ES borders, KVB↔ETCS, TVM↔ETCS) are this decoupling in operation, kin to the OBapp/gateway design. BEARING on R2 (coexistence): RSC types + RSC-EU-0 manage radio-network compatibility PER-LINE — the GSM-R→FRMCS migration will run line-by-line through this check framework, not big-bang (Germany RSC-DE removal, Spain/Portugal RSC migrations show the pattern). No decision moved (R7/R6 stay Accepted; R2 Accepted); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-25-03 (DB roaming — SIM/roaming face of the same interoperability), E-2026-07-02-15 (MORANE-2), R6/ADR-001 gateway decoupling |
| E-2026-07-25-05 | 2026-07-25 | ERA (European Union Agency for Railways) — Report "TSI CCS (EU) 2023/1695 Article 12, ERTMS compatibility and future revision", ref 2024-REP-Art.12_CCS_TSI, V2.0, 03/12/2024 (drafted Debast/Klecha, validated de Bosschere/Head of ERTMS Unit, approved Guido/Head of Railway Systems Dept). The Agency's formal Article-12 deliverable to the European Commission: mandated by CCS TSI 2023/1695 Art 12 to analyse (by 1 June 2024) "how to PHASE OUT the checks to prove the technical compatibility of on-board units with different ERTMS trackside implementations and to achieve harmonisation of engineering and operational rules for the Single European Rail Area". Paraphrased key findings: ESC (ETCS System Compatibility) + RSC (Radio System Compatibility) were introduced (amendment 2019/776 of CCS TSI 2016/919) as a TRANSITIONAL measure to instil compatibility confidence, but the large number of ESC/RSC types + multitude of checks now cause ADDITIONAL COST, LONGER PROJECT DURATION and FRAGMENTATION of the ERTMS landscape, sometimes conflicting with the EC verification process. Evidence: of tested ESC types, <10% reported actual train-track incompatibilities; most issues are PRODUCT NON-CONFORMITIES, misinterpretation of requirements, or trackside/on-board design incompatibilities — fix is usually an on-board config update. But the sector does NOT agree ESC can be quickly phased out (strong disagreement with "subsystem certificates are sufficient" and "current maturity allows removal") → no consensus, no straightforward phase-out; a stepwise action plan is needed. 10 RECOMMENDATIONS (short/med/long): (1) dedicated Working Group; (2) harmonized ESC content definition; (3) ESC-definition guideline for IMs; (4) HARMONISED TRACKSIDE ENGINEERING RULES (ERJU System Pillar SP OD working on operational harmonisation); (5) review CCS-TSI conformity-assessment requirements (NoBo §6.3 tables); (6) better ESC coverage by SUBSET-076; (7.1) spec-error-correction via Agency questionnaires (CCS TSI §7.2.10); (7.2) removal of partial-fulfilment clause (transition until ≥2030, Appendix B Table B1.2 line 16); (8) strengthen NoBo/AsBo focus on product deviations; (9) continuous training; (10) CCS national-rules cleaning-up. Ranked key enablers for phase-out: (1) more harmonisation in ERTMS trackside engineering; (2) fully CCS-TSI-compliant ERTMS ICs; (3) full application of the error-correction process; (4) harmonised track-train integration test spec; (5) ERTMS-IC authorisation process | Agency primary report (EU Agency for Railways; formal CCS-TSI Article-12 deliverable to the European Commission; recommendations = analysis/proposals, not binding) | ERA — 2024-REP-Art.12_CCS_TSI V2.0 (era.europa.eu); file "2024-rep-art.12_ccs_tsi_v2.0_0.pdf", Download folder | A | R7 (+ R6 check-/re-qualification-burden reduction; R2 partial-fulfilment 2030 transition) | validate | A — PRIMARY: an official EU-Agency-for-Railways report, named drafters/validators/approvers, versioned (V2.0), the Agency's FORMAL Article-12 deliverable to the European Commission — same source class as the ERA ESC/RSC register (E-2026-07-25-04) and MORANE-2 (E-2026-07-02-15). CAVEATS: (1) it is ANALYSIS + RECOMMENDATIONS, explicitly a "starting point for further discussions", NOT binding regulation — the 10 recommendations are proposals, not enacted requirements (mark as ERA position, not law); (2) V2.0 dated 03/12/2024 (~1.5 yr old) — verify whether the Commission has acted / a later revision exists before load-bearing use; (3) subject is ETCS + GSM-R-radio compatibility; FRMCS is not the topic (RSC-radio only, Annex 5). VALIDATES R7 (interoperability + CCS-TSI conformance, Accepted · ADR-001) with STRATEGIC context the type-register (E-04) lacks: the CCS-TSI compatibility regime FRMCS conformance sits within is itself TRANSITIONAL and formally slated for PHASE-OUT toward harmonised trackside engineering — "conform to CCS TSI" is conformance to a moving, self-reforming target, and the Single-European-Rail-Area endgame is harmonised engineering rules (ERJU System Pillar SP OD), not perpetual per-line compatibility checks. STRONG BEARING on R6 (don't re-qualify ETCS on every transport change, Accepted · ADR-001): the entire report is about REDUCING the compatibility-check burden — and its evidence sharpens R6, because <10% of tested ESC types found real incompatibilities while most "issues" were product non-conformities / spec-misinterpretation → the re-qualification burden R6 guards against is largely catching product/interpretation defects, not genuine transport incompatibilities, which is the case FOR the OBapp/gateway-decoupling + harmonisation direction (recs 4/5/6/8, error-correction process, SUBSET-076). LIGHT BEARING on R2 (coexistence): the partial-fulfilment-clause removal has a transition until ≥2030 (Appendix B Table B1.2 line 16) — a dated marker in the same decade-long window as the GSM-R→FRMCS coexistence. No decision moved (R7/R6 stay Accepted; R2 Accepted); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-25-04 (ESC/RSC type register — the operational face of this policy analysis), E-2026-07-25-03 (DB roaming), E-2026-07-02-15 (MORANE-2), R6/ADR-001 gateway decoupling; ERJU System Pillar also an ADR-012 sector interface |
| E-2026-07-25-06 | 2026-07-25 | ERA (European Union Agency for Railways) — Report "On the implementation of ETCS system compatibility (ESC) and radio system compatibility (RSC)", ref ESCRSC Art11a, V1.1 22/03/2021 (data as of V1.0 29/05/2020 — V1.1 editorial only, no data change; drafted Domingo Ortuño/Klecha/Hernández Fernández, validated Ferrari/HoU ERTMS & Telematics, approved Guido/Head of Railways System Dept). The Agency's FIRST ESC/RSC report — the formal CCS-TSI Art 11.1a deliverable to the European Commission: mandated to report (by 1 June 2020) on ESC/RSC implementation + "an assessment of the differing types of ESC and RSC, and the potential for reducing the underlying technical divergences". FINDINGS (§6) name early problems: transition from national rules (NNTRs); ESC/RSC content treated as trackside-SUPPLIER property; LACK OF HARMONISATION of ESC laboratory facilities; LACK OF CONFIDENCE in lab testing vs track testing; NoBo role; register status; and MISUSE of ESC/RSC. This is the ORIGIN-POINT document of the ESC/RSC set and completes the arc: 2020 IMPLEMENTATION baseline (THIS, Art 11a) → 2024 PHASE-OUT analysis (E-05, Art 12) → 2026 LIVE register (E-04). CAVEATS — STRONGEST "dated" of the three: data as of 29/05/2020 (V1.1 22/03/2021 editorial only, no data change) = ~6 yr old, a snapshot of an IMMATURE state (most types "reserved"/"pending" in 2020); its type-status data is ENTIRELY SUPERSEDED by the 2026 register (E-04) — VALUE IS HISTORICAL/BASELINE, not current state; do NOT cite its 2020 type counts as current. Also findings/proposed-actions, not binding. VALIDATES R7 (interoperability + CCS-TSI conformance, Accepted · ADR-001) at the origin: ESC/RSC began (2019/776) as a divergence-confidence measure and even in 2020 the Agency was mandated to assess "reducing the underlying technical divergences" — the phase-out trajectory (E-05) was built into the concept from the start. BEARING on R6 (don't re-qualify ETCS on every transport change, Accepted · ADR-001): the 2020 findings pre-figure the 2024 recommendations — lab-harmonisation gaps, low confidence in lab vs track testing, ESC/RSC-content-as-supplier-property and MISUSE are the same burden/fragmentation problems R6's decoupling guards against, documented at the regime's birth. LIGHT R2 (coexistence): NNTR-transition + temporary ERATV old-procedure code = early coexistence mechanics. No decision moved (R7/R6 Accepted; R2 Accepted); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-25-05 (Art-12 phase-out report — the follow-up this seeds), E-2026-07-25-04 (live register), E-2026-07-25-03 (DB roaming), E-2026-07-02-15 (MORANE-2) |
| E-2026-07-25-07 | 2026-07-25 | UNISIG — ERTMS/ETCS SUBSET-078 "Failure Modes and Effects Analysis for the Interface to/from an Adjacent RBC — in Application Level 2", Issue 3.5.0, 07-05-2024 (Baseline 4 release version; developed + released by UNISIG — approvals Alstom, AZD, CAF, Hitachi Rail STS, MERMEC, Siemens, Thales; a mandatory ERTMS spec in the CCS TSI Annex A set). Primary normative SAFETY-ANALYSIS document: a formal FMEA that systematically evaluates each functional failure of the MANDATORY RBC-RBC interface (Radio Block Centre handover to an adjacent RBC) in ETCS Level 2, assesses its effect on the ETCS system + train operation (assuming no other simultaneous failure), and assigns a SEVERITY based on impact on PASSENGER SAFETY — failures not affecting passenger safety are classed as RAM issues and not analysed further. Scope: handover-RBC→accepting-RBC messages (Pre-Announcement, Route Related Information Request, Announcement, Cancellation, RRI Confirmation, Train Data, Train Running Number, Safe consist length for SM) + accepting→handover (RRI, Request for RRI Confirmation, Taking Over Responsibility, Cancellation, SM RRI) + Acknowledgement. Built on SUBSET-039 (RBC-RBC handover FIS) + SUBSET-098 (RBC-RBC safe communication interface). Notes the analysis must be REFINED in the application-specific safety case (national RRI rules out of scope) and assumes system-version X=3 RBCs | Normative technical standard / safety analysis (UNISIG ERTMS/ETCS SUBSET; CCS-TSI Annex A mandatory-spec set) | UNISIG — ERTMS/ETCS SUBSET-078 Issue 3.5.0 (07-05-2024); file "index20_subset-078 v350.pdf", Download folder | A | R11 (safety-case FMEA discipline · ADR-004) (+ R5 ETCS-L2 RBC-RBC handover; R7/R6 mandatory CCS-TSI Annex A interoperability spec) | validate | A — PRIMARY normative standard: an official UNISIG ERTMS/ETCS SUBSET (Issue 3.5.0, Baseline 4), part of the CCS-TSI Annex A mandatory-spec set — the authoritative technical document for the RBC-RBC interface FMEA, not vendor marketing (multi-vendor consortium output, adopted normatively via the TSI). Same source class as ETSI/UIC/3GPP standards on file (ETSI TS 103 147, E-2026-07-01-09; UIC FRMCS SRS, E-2026-07-12-02). CAVEATS: (1) UNISIG (industry-consortium)-authored, though normative-by-TSI-adoption; (2) NARROW SCOPE — one interface (RBC↔adjacent-RBC handover), ETCS-L2, application-layer safety analysis; (3) it explicitly says the FMEA must be REFINED in the application-specific safety case (national RRI rules + X≠3 RBC version combinations out of scope) — a reference method, not a complete safety case. VALIDATES the SAFETY-ANALYSIS DISCIPLINE behind R11 (keep the safety case certifiable, Proposed — load-bearing · ADR-004) and ADR-004 (SIL-4 boundary / EN 50129 freedom-from-interference + composition): SUBSET-078 is a concrete PRIMARY instance of the formal FMEA method — enumerate each functional failure, assess its effect assuming no other failure, classify by passenger-safety severity, separate SAFETY from RAM — that the certified SIL-4 core R11 protects is built from; methodological design-basis grounding, kin to the RCA/OCORA + DB/Siemens SIL4-DC references cited for R11 (E-2026-07-02-01/-03). DOES NOT move R11's status: this is ETCS-DOMAIN reference material, NOT the engagement's own verifying safety-case evidence (independence/FFI analysis, ISA/NoBo for the oversight-layer boundary) — that is still to be produced (matrix R11 note stands). ALSO a mandatory CCS-TSI Annex A INTEROPERABILITY spec → light corroboration of R7/R6 (standardized RBC-RBC interface = interoperability without per-project re-qualification) and R5 (ETCS-L2 RBC-RBC handover is core L2 infrastructure). METHODOLOGICAL KIN to the failure-mode discipline the engagement champions (ADR-007 failure analysis; PR11 silent-fault / PR15 feature-equivalence): the FMEA's message loss/corruption/wrong-sequence → safety-effect reasoning is the same systematic failure-mode thinking the silent-fault/failover thesis applies at the NETWORK/bearer layer — an ANALOGY across layers (RBC application-message FMEA ≠ bearer silent-failover), mark as methodological kinship, not a direct bearer finding. Bearer-agnostic at the application layer — the RBC-RBC handover safety logic carries into the FRMCS target, so the discipline transfers. No decision moved (R11 stays Proposed; R5/R7/R6 Accepted); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-12-04 (Vitale runtime-verification — same methodological-grounding class), ADR-004 (SIL-4 boundary), ADR-007 (failure-injection testing), R11 design-basis refs E-2026-07-02-01/-03 |

Chapter 5 = "Monitoring of Quality of Service during Operation" (Phase 2/3) — ongoing operational QoS monitoring, distinct from Phase-1 acceptance. | E-2026-07-25-10 | 2026-07-25 | ETSI TR 103 134 V1.1.1 (2013-03), "Railway Telecommunications (RT); GSM-R in support of EC Mandate M/486 EN on Urban Rail" (ref DTR/RT-0028; produced by ETSI TC Railway Telecommunications). Informative ETSI Technical Report positioning GSM-R as a candidate to harmonise URBAN RAIL (metro/tram/light rail) systems under EC Mandate M/486 EN. Paraphrased in-scope content: describes GSM-R (EIRENE-based) architecture, services (P2P, VBS/VGCS, eMLPP, functional/location addressing, ATC bearer), QoS (handover success ≥99.5%, ISDN-equivalent speech), standardized frequency bands, and interference-robustness (blocking/desensitization table vs TETRA/UMTS/LTE). Notable resilience claims: §5.3 — "the end-to-end chain between the ATC computer and the train has no single point of failure" (internal node redundancy + double-coverage via 2 BTS sources); §5.5.4 "Enhanced robustness" — automatic disaster-recovery being standardized so a backup Core Network Equipment "would replace faulty equipment WITHOUT ANY HUMAN INTERVENTION", extendable to the Base Station Subsystem. Scale: GSM-R in 8 countries, >68,000 km EU tracks (+ non-EU: India, China, Australia, etc.). ONE USEFUL FOOTNOTE (why logged, not discarded): the TR's 2013 assertions — "no single point of failure" end-to-end (§5.3) and automatic disaster-recovery replacing faulty equipment "WITHOUT ANY HUMAN INTERVENTION" (§5.5.4) — are exactly the STANDARDS-ERA CONFIDENCE the 23-Jun-2026 outage DEFEATED: redundancy existed and was designed for automatic switchover, yet a SILENT fault meant the failover never engaged and recovery was MANUAL (E-2026-06-27-01/-03). A dated primary data-point for the PR11 "asserted-vs-proven redundancy" gap and the auto-failover-was-always-the-standard thread (kin E-2026-07-01-09). LIGHT R14 kin: the interference-robustness table (GSM-R blocking margins vs TETRA/UMTS/LTE) is background for the jamming/interference-resilience thread. No decision moved (R3 stays open; PR11 unchanged); no matrix/ADR change. Do NOT cite its urban-rail-mandate framing as relevant to the mainline transition |

| E-2026-07-25-12 | 2026-07-25 | Quattron Management Consulting (Potthoff / Döring / Becka / Lossau) — "Herausforderungen bei der Migration von GSM-R zu FRMCS" / "Challenges in GSM-R to FRMCS migration", SIGNAL + DRAHT (Signalling + Datacommunication) 112 (3/2020) pp. 6-12 (DVV Media Group; bilingual DE/EN; homepage reprint authorized for Quattron). Consultancy overview article on the GSM-R→FRMCS migration challenge. Paraphrased: §1 GSM-R rollout retrospective — UIC spec 1996, 32 European railways committed 1997; Germany ~a decade to deploy (~24,000 km equipped, ~7,000 sites, ~3,000 km fibre, ~8,300 vehicles converted, ~32,000 regulatory approval acts, ~50,000 staff trained, with Quattron support). §2 FRMCS = 5G-based (5G preferred over LTE/4G for ATO low-latency/localization); possible sharing of commercial 5G infrastructure + passenger-5G synergies in passive infra; FRMCS requirement set (marketable/vendor-independent-interoperable products, long-term support, flexible certification, availability ≥ GSM-R, resistance to interference AND EXTERNAL ATTACKS, robust durable elements, seamless ETCS voice+data migration); UIC 3 working groups (Functions / Architecture & Technology / Spectrum); UIC schedule forecast — FRMCS specs+validation by 2024, first TSI renewal 2022, second TSI end-2024. §3 FIVE KEY CHALLENGES: (1) implementation strategy ("think from the end forwards", incremental not big-bang, FRMCS-compatibility as vehicle network-access criterion; commercial-network use via network slicing "somewhat doubtful from the point of view of railway safety"); (2) infrastructure rollout (robust FRMCS core installed+tested first, then network parts; early certification/approval-authority involvement); (3) vehicle rollout & terminal procurement (retrofit planning; distinguish vehicle-fixed vs PORTABLE terminals — portables procured LATE to avoid obsolescence); (4) service migration (CS→PS; all app interfaces need adaptation + new certifications; ETCS/ATO + emergency/group/location-dependent calls re-certified per local config); (5) personnel qualification (parallel operation risks "cannibalisation" of GSM-R staff). §4 early PROGRAM STRUCTURE (sub-programs + steering/program committees incl. external stakeholders). Fig-1 migration timeline 2020-2035 (source ERA CCRCC 2019 + Quattron): parallel GSM-R+FRMCS ops, GSM-R phase-out ~2035. Cites Brand/Nänni SIGNAL+DRAHT 08/2019 [5] + Quattron's own 2018 Bavaria Konzeptstudie [4] | Consultancy thought-leadership in a specialist trade journal (Quattron Mgmt Consulting; SIGNAL+DRAHT / DVV Media; overview, promotional-adjacent) | Quattron Management Consulting — SIGNAL + DRAHT 112 (3/2020) pp.6-12; file "Herausforderungen-bei-der-Migration-von-GSM-R-zu-FRMCS.pdf", Download folder | B | R2 (incremental coexistence migration) + R13 (fleet/vehicle/terminal retrofit) (+ R1 obsolescence; R7 interoperability/certification; R5 5G-for-ATO; R4/R14 commercial-network-slicing-safety; PR14 terminal-obsolescence) | watch | B — consultancy THOUGHT-LEADERSHIP (true but promotional), not primary: authored entirely by Quattron Management Consulting (a rail migration-consultancy positioning itself as the FRMCS-migration expert — repeated "with Quattron support", cites its own 2018 Bavaria Konzeptstudie [4]) and published/reprinted in the SIGNAL + DRAHT trade journal. Sits at the B/C boundary — trade-journal VENUE (C-ish) but single-consultancy AUTHORSHIP + promotional framing (B); tiered B per the repo's Capgemini-consultancy precedent. NOT A (derivative overview, synthesises UIC/ERA/ETSI status + cites Brand/Nänni [5] — not primary spec/research). CAVEATS: (1) promotional (consultancy self-positioning); (2) 2020-DATED — forecasts now OVERTAKEN ("FRMCS specs+validation by 2024", TSI 2022/end-2024): verify vs current (FRMCS SRS v1.0.0 E-2026-07-12-02; CCS TSI 2023/1695 E-2026-07-25-04/-05); (3) GSM-R-rollout scale figures (~24,000 km / 8,300 vehicles / 32,000 approvals / 50,000 staff) consultancy-stated, plausible but unverified. WHY WATCH: corroborates — with no decision-grade NEW fact — the incremental-coexistence-migration thesis (R2, Accepted) and the fleet/vehicle/terminal retrofit challenge (R13, Open · ADR-009) the repo already holds from stronger primaries (E-13; ADR-009). USEFUL NUGGETS: (a) HISTORICAL GSM-R rollout scale as an R1/R13 order-of-magnitude anchor; (b) PORTABLE-terminal "procure LATE to avoid obsolescence" = a concrete PR14 tactic; (c) "commercial-network use via network slicing somewhat DOUBTFUL from the railway-safety POV" — light R4 + R14 marker, echoing ENISA telecom-dependency (E-2026-07-20-01) + SBA/slice-isolation (E-2026-07-24-01); (d) "resistance to interference AND external attacks" FRMCS requirement (light R14). PROVENANCE: same SIGNAL+DRAHT venue + Quattron orbit as Brand/Nänni [5] — a third-party (non-engagement) consultancy, distinct from VPnetConsulting. No decision moved; no matrix/ADR change |

"Converged Solution Designed for Railway" spans GSM-R/2G, ETCS/ATO, MCX/IMS, 4G/5G core+RAN monitoring with "cyber inter-domain correlations" + umbrella monitoring/drive-test/KPI/troubleshooting. CYBERSECURITY framing: cites CRA + NIS2 progressively entering into force; EVOIA Assure "cybersecurity features" claimed — "no exploitable known vulnerabilities", CVSS-7 fast security update, ciphered data transit, immutable OS, surface-attack limitation, protection from unauthorized access (VENDOR CLAIMS). Long-term MAINTENANCE support contract (min 8-yr term): maintain installed GSM-R/ETCS licenses, QATS/NetProbe→EVOIA migration, HW availability/repair/replacement to 2040 incl. cover for hardware-manufacturer end-of-maintenance | Vendor marketing brochure (VIAVI Solutions; test/assurance product promotion; promotional) | VIAVI Solutions — brochure "Long-term Commitment to GSM-R and the Migration to FRMCS" (June 2024, gsmrtofmrcs-br-rlw-nse-ae); file "long-term-commitment-gsm-r-and-migration-frmcs-2040-brochures-en.pdf", Download folder | B | R12 (monitoring/assurance tooling · ADR-002) + R1 (GSM-R obsolescence) (+ R2 ~2040 coexistence figure; R14 GSM-R vulns / EVOIA-cyber / CRA-NIS2; R13/PR13/PR14 EOL long-term-support) | watch | B — VENDOR-PROMOTIONAL (true but promotional): a 4-page VIAVI Solutions MARKETING BROCHURE ("Trust VIAVI", EVOIA product pitch) — first-party vendor material about its OWN product/commitment, so B not D; the most purely promotional item logged to date. NOT A/C. DISCOUNT SUPERLATIVES per CLAUDE.md — the EVOIA Assure security claims ("no exploitable known vulnerabilities", "cyber-proof", "immutable OS") are UNVERIFIABLE VENDOR CLAIMS, not evidence; do NOT cite them. WHY WATCH: promotional, no decision-grade new fact; corroborates (in a marketing register) the GSM-R-obsolescence premise (R1) the repo already holds from primary (DB Zustandsbericht E-2026-06-24-19; obsolescence driver E-13). No decision moved; no matrix/ADR change. VENDOR-LANDSCAPE (R8/PR7): VIAVI = the test/assurance segment of the FRMCS ecosystem, distinct from the core vendors (Nokia/Ericsson/Kontron) |
| E-2026-07-26-02 | 2026-07-26 | UIC — "ETCS in PS-mode GPRS/EGPRS Guideline", Ref O-8664, v1.0.0, approved 02/2016 (content based on v0.28, 17.12.2014; UIC ERTMS/GSM-R Operators Group; authors I. Wendler (UIC/SBB), S. Guillemaut (Kapsch CarrierCom), M. Lauwers (Nokia); reviewed TEN-T 3rd Call WP Leader; approved UIC ERIG Chairman R. Sarfati; ACCESS: Public; property of UIC). Primary UIC operators-group GUIDELINE for deploying ETCS Level 2 in PACKET-SWITCHED (PS) mode over GPRS/EGPRS on GSM-R. Paraphrased: motivates PS-mode by GSM-R CS-mode CAPACITY BOTTLENECKS in dense hubs (narrow 4 MHz GSM-R band; CS-mode dedicates a full timeslot per data call) — PS-mode (IP-based GPRS/EGPRS) shares a traffic channel across users, freeing/reallocating unused resources. Specifies mandatory (M)/conditional (C)/optional (O) network extensions, engineering recommendations and 3GPP GPRS parameter settings for GPRS/EGPRS as the ETCS bearer. Covers: GSM/GPRS reference architecture + protocol stack (Um/Abis/Gb/Gn-Gp); IP addressing (OBU IP pool, RBC IP addressing); mobile classes; channel coding + retransmission (incremental redundancy); GSM-R radio network architecture (single/dual radio layer) + planning; ETCS application characteristics; Radio Link Operation (Temporary Block Flow); GPRS mobility (cell update/change/reselection); §5 QUALITY OF SERVICE (ETCS QoS Profile subscription, BSS QoS mgmt, packet-core + external-IP QoS, roaming QoS, QoS requirements); power control; Domain Name Services (GNS + DNS for ETCS); §8 misc — eMLPP interaction, session mgmt, location/routing-area design, network modes of operation, VGCS/VBS interaction, §8.7 SECURITY + Table 8-3 "Security Advices", Signalling-System Handover (RBC handover national + cross-border); Annex 3GPP parameters (BSS/GMM/SM/BSSGP) | Normative operators guideline (UIC ERTMS/GSM-R Operators Group; Public; industry-co-authored) | UIC — ETCS in PS-mode GPRS/EGPRS Guideline O-8664 v1.0.0 (02/2016); file "o-8664-1.0.0_etcs_in_ps-mode_gprs-egprs_guideline.pdf", Download folder | A | R5 (packet-native bearer for ETCS) (+ R6 bearer-coupled ETCS engineering / OBapp decoupling; R12 ETCS QoS profile · companion to O-2475; R4 QoS/mobility/handover resilience; R7 interoperable deployment + cross-border RBC handover; R14 §8.7 security) | validate | A — PRIMARY: a UIC ERTMS/GSM-R Operators-Group guideline, versioned (v1.0.0), Public, property of UIC, approved by the UIC ERIG Chairman (R. Sarfati) — same source class as the UIC QoS test spec O-2475 (E-2026-07-25-08) and the UIC FRMCS SRS (E-2026-07-12-02). Industry-co-authored (Kapsch CarrierCom + Nokia named) — NORMAL for UIC/ERTMS operator guidelines (cf. UNISIG SUBSET-078, E-2026-07-25-07); UIC-property, UIC-approved, NOT vendor marketing. CAVEATS: (1) DATED — v1.0.0 2016 (content 2014); (2) GSM-R-era PACKET mode (GPRS/EGPRS) — its SPECIFIC engineering (GPRS parameters, TBF, channel coding) is GSM-R-bound and does NOT carry to the FRMCS 5G bearer; VALUE is the ETCS-over-packet PRINCIPLE + QoS discipline that FRMCS inherits at a higher level. VALIDATES R5 (packet-native bearer, Accepted · ADR-001) at the ORIGIN: the packet-native-bearer direction is NOT an FRMCS novelty — ETCS was ALREADY evolving from CS-mode to PS-mode ON GSM-R (GPRS/EGPRS), DRIVEN by CS-mode capacity limits; FRMCS's packet-native 5G bearer is the CONTINUATION. STRONG BEARING on R6 (Accepted · ADR-001): O-8664 is a concrete instance of ETCS tightly COUPLED to a specific bearer's engineering — exactly the bearer-coupling the OBapp/TOBA gateway-decoupling (R6) exists to END. BEARING on R12 (Proposed · ADR-002): §5 ETCS QoS Profile + BSS/packet-core QoS is the REQUIREMENTS companion to O-2475's QoS-measurement (E-2026-07-25-08). BEARING on R4 (Open): QoS, retransmission, mobility, RBC handover; R7 (Accepted): interoperable ETCS-PS + cross-border RBC handover (§8.8.3). LIGHT R14 (§8.7 Security — GSM-R-era, superseded by the FRMCS/CRA regime, ADR-012). No decision moved (R5/R6/R7 Accepted; R12 Proposed; R4 Open); no matrix/ADR change. | E-2026-07-26-03 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 103 066 V1.1.2 (2012-04), "Railway Telecommunications (RT); Rel-4 Core Network requirements for GSM-R" (ref RTS/RT-00019; keywords BICC, R-GSM; produced by ETSI TC Railway Telecommunications). PRIMARY normative ETSI Technical Specification describing the Rel-4 Core Network architecture — the Bearer Independent Core Network (BICN) — for OPTIONAL application on GSM-R railway networks. Paraphrased: legacy GSM-R CN relies on Rel-99; this TS specifies the Rel-4 BICN as an OPTIONAL evolution (explicitly "does NOT mandate Rel-4 to become the reference architecture for railways — baseline remains Rel-99"), CS-DOMAIN only. Rel-4 BICN concept: the MSC entity SPLIT into an MSC-Server (signalling only) + a CS-MGW (Circuit-Switched Media Gateway, user data) via the Mc reference point (GMSC likewise → GMSC-Server + CS-MGW; Nc between servers, Nb between media gateways; BICC = Bearer Independent Call Control) — enabling bearer-INDEPENDENT core transport (ATM or IP). Covers reference architecture (Rel-99 + Rel-4), interfaces (A/B/C/D/E/F/G + Mc/Nc/Nb), legacy procedures, legacy services (GSM basic, VGCS, VBS, supplementary) and §7.3 RAILWAY-SPECIFIC services: Confirmation of High Priority Call (REC), Functional Addressing, Location Dependent Addressing, presentation of functional numbers, Railway Emergency Call. CAVEATS → WHY WATCH not validate: (1) DATED 2012, GSM-R-era Rel-4 (EDITIONS ON FILE: V1.1.1 + V1.1.2 (2012-04) — V1.1.2 is the current/superseding edition, third-digit editorial bump, same technical content; cite V1.1.2); (2) OPTIONAL by its own words — "does NOT mandate Rel-4; baseline remains Rel-99" → a deployment VARIANT some operators chose; (3) CS-DOMAIN only; (4) its BICN/MSC-Server split is GSM-R-era CS-core and does NOT carry to the FRMCS 5G SA core (5GC/IMS) — background/completeness. VALUE: (a) R3 core-architecture context — rounds out the GSM-R core-network standards set behind the 23-Jun estate (the MSC-Server + Media-Gateway split + bearer-independent ATM/IP transport is the CS-domain step toward an IP core; NB it protects nothing about the transmission/distribution layer where DB located the fault — do NOT present BICN as the redundancy that failed, same guard as TS 123 236); (b) PR15 — §7.3 documents the CN-level RAILWAY-SPECIFIC service set (REC, Functional Addressing, Location-Dependent Addressing, Railway Emergency Call) that MCX must reproduce equivalently (kin UIC test-plan E-2026-07-25-11, EIRENE MI set E-2026-07-02-30/-31); (c) LIGHT R5 — BICN bearer-independence is an early IP-evolution marker (CS-domain analogue to O-8664's PS evolution, E-2026-07-26-02); (d) R1/R7 — GSM-R standards lineage. CAVEATS → WHY WATCH: (1) DATED — Rel-99 (1999), GSM Phase 2+ (but this IS the frozen-baseline version EN 301 515 pins, so vintage is by-design, not staleness); (2) COMMERCIAL GSM, pinned-for-railway not railway-bespoke; (3) PRE-3GPP-ALIGNMENT ETSI-SMG-era spec (same frozen-base character as EN 300 904 / ETS 300 585) — its 3GPP successor is TS 48.008 (BSSMAP), NOT on file (living-delta continuation; same GSM-0x→3GPP-4x renumbering as GSM 03.68→TS 43.068, E-2026-07-05-06); NB published TS (not a draft, unlike EN 300 904). VALUE (grounds an established interoperability fact): the A-interface is THE mixed-vendor GSM-R interop seam — the TEN plans PROVED NSN-NSS + Kapsch-BSS and Kapsch-NSS + NSN-BSS integrate AT THIS interface without simulators (E-2026-07-06-03, A); TS 100 590 is the PRIMARY SPEC behind that interop. It is also the seam that makes DB InfraGO's REGIONALLY-SPLIT MULTI-VENDOR estate work — the Nokia (south) / Siemens+Huawei (north) BSS meeting the Kapsch/Kontron Rel-4 core (E-2026-06-29-02 C; finding-dbinfrago-rel4-core-architecture.md) all interoperate across this standardised A-interface. So it grounds R7 (interoperability, Accepted · ADR-001) at the essential-pinned-spec level, and R8/PR7 (the standardised seam is what ENABLES the multi-vendor estate — interoperability as the counterweight to lock-in). ALSO relevant to the FAILOVER-TRIGGER question (DIAG-009): the BSSMAP RESET procedure + SCCP connection management here are the A-interface-level failure-RECOVERY machinery at the BSS↔core seam (recovery after failure, not the failover trigger itself). LIGHT R3: the A-interface is the BSS↔core connection in the core-architecture picture (equipment-map §4/§5). DOES NOT move a status: primary text behind facts already A-established (E-2026-07-05-03 pinned set; E-2026-07-06-03 mixed-vendor interop) — R7 Accepted, R8 Recommended, R1 unchanged; no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-06-03 (TEN mixed-vendor A-interface interop — the proof this spec underpins), E-2026-07-05-03 (EN 301 515 — pins this as essential), E-2026-07-26-07 (EN 300 904/GSM 02.02 — references this), E-2026-06-29-02 + finding-dbinfrago-rel4-core-architecture.md (DB multi-vendor estate joined across this A-interface), equipment-map §4-5 |

| E-2026-07-26-11 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 103 764 V1.1.0 (2025-11), "Rail Telecommunications (RT); Future Railway Mobile Communication System (FRMCS); System Architecture" (FINAL DRAFT, submitted for ETSI Vote phase / SRdAP; ref DTS/RT-0082; ETSI TC Railway Telecommunications; 26 pp). PRIMARY normative ETSI spec defining the FRMCS high-level SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE (on-network mode). Paraphrased: two strata inside the FRMCS System — SERVICE STRATUM (centred on the 3GPP Mission-Critical Communications (MCX) framework; decouples applications from transport) + TRANSPORT STRATUM (connectivity, mobility, QoS, policy, auth); the RAILWAY APPLICATION STRATUM is explicitly OUTSIDE the FRMCS architecture. Key principle #1: "maximizing the decoupling between the strata to facilitate independent evolution of the Transport and/or Service Stratum without mandating an evolution of the Application Stratum". Components (ch5): FRMCS Domain (Transport Domain + Service Domain), On-Board FRMCS, FRMCS-capable Handheld, FRMCS-capable Object, FRMCS Trackside Gateway, On-Board + Infrastructure FRMCS Multipath Functions, EIRENE (GSM-R-based) System, Non-EIRENE non-FRMCS System. Reference points: OBapp (on-board app), TSapp (trackside app), TSctrl (controller); Local Binding for authentication; tight-coupled (standard 3GPP MCX reference points) vs loose-coupled (OBapp API) application modes. Four APPLICATION TYPES (from UIC FRMCS SRS): I interoperable-IM (ATP/ATO/REC/driver-controller voice), II non-interoperable-IM, III interoperable-RU, IV non-interoperable-RU (e.g. TCMS). Modularity: stand-alone / interconnected international network / GSM-R-interworking / non-EIRENE-non-FRMCS deployments. Procedures (ch6): FRMCS Start/Close of Operation, inter-FRMCS-Domain transitions (per app Type), GSM-R↔FRMCS Domain transition. Cybersecurity (§4.2.5): multi-layered across Transport + Service Strata; at the periphery the OBapp/TSapp reference points (UIC FFFIS-7950) require Local Binding establishing a secured TLS-backed mutually-authenticated control-plane link. IP addressing/routing ch8. Normative refs: TS 103 765-1/-2/-3/-4 (Building Blocks: Transport/Service/On-Board/Trackside), TS 103 792 (GSM-R interworking); informative: UIC FRMCS FFFIS-7950, FIS-7970, SRS, ETSI TR 103 791 (terminology) | Standard (primary — ETSI TS; final draft, current) | ETSI TS 103 764 V1.1.0 (2025-11), DTS/RT-0082 (etsi.org); file "ts_103764v010100va.pdf", Download folder | A | R6 (strata decoupling — the core FRMCS architecture principle) (+ R5 MCX service layer; R2 GSM-R interworking/domain transition; R7 inter-FRMCS-domain interoperability; R4 FRMCS Multipath / multi-bearer; R14 multi-layered cybersecurity + Local Binding/TLS) | validate | A — PRIMARY, CURRENT normative standard: an ETSI TC-RT Technical Specification (DTS/RT-0082, 2025-11) — the FIRST and most current primary FRMCS SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE spec in the repo, same source class as TS 103 147 / EIRENE / the FRMCS SRS (E-2026-07-12-02). CAVEAT: "FINAL DRAFT" (submitted for the ETSI Vote phase / SRdAP) — version + structure solid, but confirm against the published TS before clause-level citation (same discipline as the EN 300 904 / EN 301 515 draft flags); v1.1.0 Nov-2025, very fresh. PUBLISHED AS V1.1.1 (2026-01): the released edition of this V1.1.0 final draft is now on file (ETSI TS 103 764 V1.1.1, published, 26 pp — same structure) — draft caveat RESOLVED; cite V1.1.1 as the published edition (third-digit publication bump). THE NORMATIVE REALIZATION OF ADR-001's TARGET ARCHITECTURE: everything ADR-001 chose appears here as the actual ETSI FRMCS spec — Service(MCX)/Transport strata split with the Application Stratum OUTSIDE the system (= R6 gateway-decoupling, stated as architecture principle #1 "maximizing decoupling between strata"), OBapp/TSapp reference points, tight-vs-loose-coupled app modes, FRMCS Multipath (R4), GSM-R interworking + GSM-R↔FRMCS domain transition (R2), multi-layered cybersecurity + Local Binding/TLS (R14). VALIDATES R6 (don't re-qualify ETCS on every transport change, Accepted · ADR-001) at the strongest level yet — the strata-decoupling principle R6/OBapp rests on IS this spec's #1 design principle, now A-tier ETSI-normative rather than resting on 5GRAIL/vendor material (E-2026-07-25-09, E-2026-07-01-10/-12/-14); and R5 (MCX service layer, Accepted), R2 (interworking, Accepted), R7 (inter-domain interoperability, Accepted), R4 (multipath/fail-soft, Open — the Multipath Function is the multi-bearer mechanism R4 wants), R14 (cybersecurity, Open — Local Binding/TLS control-plane security, ties ADR-012). DOES NOT flip a status: confirmatory primary grounding of already-Accepted architecture decisions + the Open R4/R14 directions (R5/R6/R7/R2 stay Accepted; R4/R14 Open; ADR-001 Proposed) — no matrix/ADR change. STRONG CITATION-UPDATE CANDIDATE for ADR-001 (its references cite "ERA FRMCS SRS (AT-7800) / TOBA FRS" + "FRS/SRS V1 finalised, V2 in flight" — this current ETSI architecture TS should be added as the normative architecture source; flagged, not applied). REFERENCE-TREE: opens a new sub-tree — normative refs TS 103 765-1/-2/-3/-4 (Building Blocks) + TS 103 792 (GSM-R interworking); informative UIC FRMCS SRS = E-2026-07-12-02 (on file). KIN: E-2026-07-12-02 (UIC FRMCS SRS — this is its ETSI architecture realization), E-2026-07-26-03 (TS 103 066 — the GSM-R Rel-4 core this is the 5G successor to), E-2026-07-25-09 (5GRAIL gateway prototypes), E-2026-07-01-10/-12/-14 (vendor FRMCS designs — now superseded by this normative spec), ADR-001, R6/gateway decoupling |
| E-2026-07-26-12 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 103 765-4 V1.1.1 (2026-01), "Rail Telecommunications (RT); FRMCS; Building Blocks and Functions; Part 4: Trackside functions and interfaces" (published ETSI TS; ref DTS/RT-0086; ETSI TC-RT; 22 pp). PRIMARY normative spec for the NON-3GPP FRMCS TRACKSIDE infrastructure — principally the FRMCS TRACKSIDE GATEWAY (the logical SYMMETRICAL counterpart to the On-Board FRMCS/TOBA at the infrastructure side; links the Transport Network to trackside Applications). Paraphrased: trackside network architecture (IWF, 5G Core, Multipath Gateway, MCx Server, FRMCS Trackside Gateway, VAS-Controller Equipment, Trackside Application). Trackside Gateway hosts FRMCS Service-Stratum functions for LOOSE-COUPLED applications (H2H/H2N models): Hosting Clients (MC service clients integrating loose-coupled apps into the MC service system), TSapp Server, Trackside Application Network NAT Function, Trackside Service NAT Function. Reference points: TSapp (to trackside applications — per UIC FFFIS-7950; a REST/HTTP-style API with /registrations, /sessions, /notifications [SSE event stream], /versions, /keepalive endpoints + registration/session/notification procedures), TAP TS (to transport core network + FRMCS Service Server; MCX control-plane + user-plane client interfaces), TSctrl (VAS-Controller Equipment). IWF = centralized interworking between MCPTT/MCData and GSM-R (per TS 103 792). Part 4 of a 5-part set (Part 1 Transport Stratum, 2 Service Stratum, 3 Train On-Board, 4 Trackside, 5 UE capabilities). Normative refs: UIC FFFIS-7950, TS 103 765-1/-2, TS 123 280 (MC common), TS 133 180 (MC security), TS 103 792 (GSM-R interworking), TS 123 379 (MCPTT), TS 123 282 (MCData), ISO 8601 | Standard (primary — ETSI TS; published, current) | ETSI TS 103 765-4 V1.1.1 (2026-01), DTS/RT-0086 (etsi.org); file "ts_10376504v010101p.pdf", Download folder | A | R6 (FRMCS Trackside Gateway = trackside realization of the OBapp/gateway decoupling) (+ R5 MCX service stratum; R2 IWF GSM-R interworking; R7 interoperability; R4 Multipath Gateway; R14 NAT / TSapp security) | validate | A — PRIMARY, PUBLISHED, CURRENT normative standard: an ETSI TC-RT Technical Specification (DTS/RT-0086, 2026-01) — PUBLISHED (not a draft, unlike the TS 103 764 architecture spec E-2026-07-26-11 which is a final draft); Part 4 of the FRMCS Building-Blocks set. Same source class as TS 103 764 / TS 103 147 / FRMCS SRS (E-2026-07-12-02). CAVEAT: narrow scope (trackside gateway functions/interfaces only). ONE OF TS 103 764's NORMATIVE REFERENCES (E-2026-07-26-11) — the building-block detail beneath the architecture. VALIDATES R6 (don't re-qualify ETCS on every transport change, Accepted · ADR-001) at the trackside building-block level: the FRMCS TRACKSIDE GATEWAY is the logical SYMMETRICAL counterpart to the On-Board FRMCS/TOBA (R6's on-board gateway) — the same gateway-decoupling pattern realized on the infrastructure side, hosting Service-Stratum functions for loose-coupled apps behind the TSapp reference point (mirror of OBapp). So R6's gateway-decoupling now has BOTH ends A-tier ETSI-normative: on-board (TOBA, via TS 103 765-3 not yet on file) + trackside (this). BEARING: R5 (MCX service stratum — MCx Server + MC service clients, Accepted); R2 (the IWF = centralized MCPTT/MCData↔GSM-R interworking per TS 103 792 — the coexistence seam, Accepted); R7 (interoperability, Accepted); R4 (Multipath Gateway integration — combinable with the Trackside Gateway, Open); R14 (Trackside Application/Service NAT Functions + TSapp security, Open — ADR-012 kin). DOES NOT flip a status: confirmatory building-block grounding of already-Accepted architecture (R5/R6/R7/R2 Accepted; R4/R14 Open; ADR-001 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. REFERENCE-TREE: fills TS 103 765-4 of the 103 765 Building-Blocks set (E-2026-07-26-11's normative refs); still missing 103 765-1 (Transport Stratum), -2 (Service Stratum), -3 (Train On-Board — the TOBA/on-board-gateway counterpart), -5 (UE), + TS 103 792 (GSM-R interworking); its own refs also open TS 123 280/379/282 (MC/MCPTT/MCData) + TS 133 180 (MC security). KIN: E-2026-07-26-11 (TS 103 764 architecture — the parent this details), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS = UIC FW-AT 7800, its informative ref), E-2026-07-25-09 (5GRAIL GCG = the Ground Comm Gateway prototype of exactly this trackside gateway), master-architecture-reference.md (GCG/trackside gateway), ADR-001/R6 |
| E-2026-07-26-13 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 103 792 V1.0.0 (2025-07), "Rail Telecommunications (RT); FRMCS; Interworking with GSM-R" (DRAFT ETSI TS, submitted for combined Public Enquiry + Vote / SRdAP; ref DTS/RT-0088; ETSI TC-RT; 64 pp). PRIMARY normative spec defining the INTERWORKING FUNCTION (IWF) between GSM-R R4 and FRMCS (3GPP Mission-Critical) — functional model, reference points, protocols and procedures enabling users/groups on the two technologies to communicate during coexistence; satisfies the interworking need studied in TR 103 768. SERVICES: Group call, Emergency group call, Point-to-point call, Text messaging. IWF reference points: IWF-1 (↔MCPTT server), IWF-2 (↔MCData server), IWF-3 (↔group management server), IWF-g1 (↔MSC server/MGCF), IWF-g2 (↔MGW), IWF-g5 (↔IP-SM-GW). IDENTITIES & ADDRESSING: mapping between EIRENE (GSM-R) addresses and FRMCS Functional Aliases (FA) — E.164/EIRENE SIP-R URIs, local address lookup + rule-based "rephrasing" (per FRMCS FRS); SIP routing GSM-R↔FRMCS; identity mapping for MCPTT private/group calls, MCData SDS, supplementary services (Presentation of Functional Number, Call Diversion, Explicit Call Transfer). GROUP CALLS (§7): initiation either side, late entry, release, TALKER CONTROL (GSM-R uplink control + dispatcher mute/unmute mapped to MCPTT floor control), media encryption, codec negotiation + AMR-WB transcoding. P2P (§8), TEXT MESSAGING SDS↔MCData/Instant-Messaging (§9). Normative refs incl. UIC FU-7120/FW-AT 7800/FIS-7970, TS 103 765-2, TS 123 228 (IMS), TS 129 379 (MCPTT-LMR interworking), TS 124 282/124 379 (MCData/MCPTT), TS 123 283 (MC-LMR interworking), TS 129 163 (IMS-CS interworking), TS 133 180 (MC security), TS 103 389 (SIP GSM-R); informative TR 103 768 (interworking study), EIRENE SRS 16.1.0 (Feb 2023) | Standard (primary — ETSI TS; draft, current) | ETSI TS 103 792 V1.0.0 (2025-07), DTS/RT-0088 (etsi.org); file "ts_103792v010000ev.pdf", Download folder | A | R2 (GSM-R↔FRMCS interworking — the coexistence seam) + PR15 (MCX feature-equivalence — now normatively specified) (+ R5 MCX; R7 interoperability; R14 media encryption / MC security) | validate | A — PRIMARY, CURRENT normative standard: an ETSI TC-RT Technical Specification (DTS/RT-0088, 2025-07) — the NORMATIVE GSM-R↔FRMCS Interworking Function (IWF) spec, same source class as TS 103 764/765-4 and the FRMCS SRS (E-2026-07-12-02). CAVEAT: DRAFT (Public Enquiry + Vote / SRdAP) — version/structure solid, confirm vs published TS before clause-level citation (same discipline as TS 103 764, E-2026-07-26-11); V1.0.0 2025-07, current. PUBLISHED AS V1.1.1 (2026-01): the released edition of this V1.0.0 Public-Enquiry/Vote draft is now on file (ETSI TS 103 792 V1.1.1, published, 64 pp — same title / ref DTS/RT-0088 / length as the draft, so structure + ToC stable) — draft caveat RESOLVED; cite V1.1.1 as the published edition. NB the V1.0.0→V1.1.1 jump may carry Public-Enquiry detail changes beyond editorial; the row's scope-level content (IWF reference points, services, identity mapping) is ToC-stable, but verify any specific clause against V1.1.1. THE most decision-relevant FRMCS-shortlist item for the ENGAGEMENT: the normative realization of the GSM-R↔FRMCS interworking the coexistence strategy (R2) and the MCX-equivalence risk (PR15) turn on; the trackside IWF (E-2026-07-26-12 §4) specified in full (reference points, EIRENE↔FA identity mapping/rephrasing, per-service interworking). VALIDATES R2 (coexistence, Accepted · ADR-001) — GSM-R↔FRMCS interworking is now NORMATIVELY SPECIFIED, not just prototyped (5GRAIL group-call interworking, E-2026-07-25-09) or vendor-in-development (Kontron IWF "standards not yet fully specified", E-2026-07-26-06). STRENGTHENS the PR15 mitigation (MCX feature-equivalence / bridge-fidelity, High): the named PR15 gaps — group/emergency calls, functional-alias, REC, talker/floor control, mute/unmute, codec/encryption — are the exact §6-§9 subject, so "equivalence validated-in-progress, 3GPP-evolving" (E-2026-07-01-10) advances to "interworking NORMATIVELY SPECIFIED (in draft)"; PR15 risk-register note updated accordingly (2026-07-26). BUT does NOT close PR15 or flip a status: (1) DRAFT, not published/mandated; (2) a spec existing ≠ equivalence PROVEN — the ADR-007(e) MCX-parity regression must still demonstrate it in test; (3) it is INTERWORKING (bridging GSM-R↔MCX during coexistence), NOT proof MCX standalone reproduces every ASCI/EIRENE (MI) safety feature (the FRS 8.1.0/SRS 16.1.0 bar, E-2026-07-02-30/-31). So R2 stays Accepted, PR15 stays a treated High risk (mitigation strengthened, not resolved); no matrix status flip. R14: media encryption + MC security (TS 133 180) at the interworking boundary (ADR-012 kin). REFERENCE-TREE: satisfies TR 103 768 (interworking study — repo-referenced, unlogged); refs the MC-interworking 3GPP set (TS 123 283, 129 379, 129 163, 124 282/379) + TS 103 389 (SIP-on-GSM-R) + TS 103 765-2 (Service Stratum) — none on file; informative EIRENE SRS 16.1.0 = the repo's cited EIRENE version (E-2026-07-02-30/-31). KIN: E-2026-07-26-11/-12 (TS 103 764 architecture + 765-4 trackside IWF — this is the IWF protocol/procedure detail), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS), E-2026-07-25-09 (5GRAIL group-call interworking prototype), E-2026-07-26-06 (Kontron IWF — vendor implementation of this), E-2026-07-01-10 (named MCX gaps), PR15/ADR-007(e), R2/ADR-001 |
| E-2026-07-26-14 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 133 501 V18.6.0 (2024-07) = 3GPP TS 33.501 Release 18 — "5G; Security architecture and procedures for 5G System" (published ETSI/3GPP TS; ref RTS/TSGS-0333501vi60; 332 pp). PRIMARY normative spec for 5G SYSTEM SECURITY — the authoritative security architecture the FRMCS 5G SA core inherits. Paraphrased scope (title + domain-level, NOT clause-verified — 332 pp, front matter read only): the 5GS security architecture (trust model + security domains: network-access, network-domain, user-domain, application-domain, SBA-domain, visibility/configurability) and procedures — primary authentication & key agreement (5G-AKA, EAP-AKA'; SEAF/AUSF/UDM/ARPF/SIDF roles); subscription-identifier privacy (SUPI concealment via SUCI); key hierarchy/derivation; NAS + AS (RRC/UP) integrity + ciphering with the NEA/NIA algorithm families; security contexts + mobility/handover security; interworking (EPS) security; SERVICE-BASED-ARCHITECTURE (SBA) security — NF-to-NF mutual authentication/authorization (TLS + OAuth2 token-based authz), SEPP (Security Edge Protection Proxy) for inter-PLMN (N32) protection; secondary authentication; network-slicing security; non-3GPP access | Standard (primary — ETSI/3GPP TS; 5G security, published, current) | ETSI TS 133 501 V18.6.0 (2024-07) = 3GPP TS 33.501 Rel-18, RTS/TSGS-0333501vi60 (etsi.org); file "ts_133501v180600p.pdf", Download folder | A | R14 (cybersecurity — the normative 5G security architecture FRMCS's 5G SA core must implement) (+ ADR-012 secure-by-design technical basis; R5 5G bearer security) | validate | A — PRIMARY, PUBLISHED, CURRENT normative standard: the authoritative 3GPP/ETSI 5G-System SECURITY architecture spec (TS 33.501 Rel-18 V18.6.0, 2024-07) — same source class as the FRMCS ETSI specs (TS 103 764/765/792) and the E-2026-07-05 3GPP-lineage set. CAVEATS: (1) GENERIC 5G, not rail-specific — but FRMCS's 5G SA core IS a 3GPP 5G system, so TS 33.501 applies by INHERITANCE (same framing as the CCS'25 5G-core paper, E-2026-07-24-01; the FRMCS SRS §15 + ETSI FRMCS specs build on 3GPP security); (2) 332 pp — characterized at SCOPE level from title + front matter + domain knowledge, NOT clause-verified (paraphrase, no clause-level citation); (3) Rel-18 — current but 5G security evolves per release. VALIDATES R14 (cybersecurity regulatory conformance, Open) + grounds ADR-012 (CRA/NIS-2 secure-by-design, Proposed) at the TECHNICAL security-architecture layer: this is the normative 5G-core security architecture FRMCS's transport/core must implement (5G-AKA authentication, SUPI/SUCI identity privacy, NAS/AS ciphering+integrity, SBA TLS/OAuth2 NF-authz, SEPP inter-PLMN protection) — the secure-by-design foundation beneath ADR-012's regulatory overlay; ADR-012 action-2 updated to cite it as the normative basis (2026-07-26). NORMATIVE COUNTERPART to the CCS'25 attack evidence (E-2026-07-24-01): TS 33.501 SPECIFIES the very controls whose OPTIONAL/omittable deployment the paper showed being skipped (N2/N4 protection; SBA authentication) — the paper's "N2/N4 IPsec often disabled" + control/user-plane bridging is what happens when TS 33.501's protections are not fully deployed; this directly substantiates ADR-012's procurement hook (MANDATE N2/N4 IPsec + SBA security, don't leave them optional). DOES NOT flip a status: technical security-architecture GROUNDING, not resolution of R14 (needs the CRA/NIS-2 conformance + change-governance of ADR-012) or ratification of ADR-012 (Proposed) — R14 stays Open, ADR-012 Proposed; no matrix status flip. REFERENCE-TREE: the security-half shortlist item behind TS 103 792 + TS 103 765-4; the CENELEC TS 50701 (railway cybersecurity) half still not on file (paywalled residual). KIN: E-2026-07-24-01 (CCS'25 5G-core attacks — the exploitation counterpart), ADR-012 (secure-by-design), E-2026-07-26-13 (TS 103 792 — refs TS 133 180 MC-security atop this), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS §15 security), E-2026-07-20-01 (ENISA 5G-security-controls thread), R14/PR16 |
| E-2026-07-26-15 | 2026-07-26 | CENELEC CLC/TS 50701:2021 "Railway applications – Cybersecurity" (July 2021; approved by CENELEC 2021-05-11; prepared by CLC/TC 9X; English version), accessed via the SLOVENIAN NATIONAL ADOPTION SIST-TS CLC/TS 50701:2021 (identical to CLC/TS 50701) — this file is an iTeh STANDARD PREVIEW (watermarked): only FRONT MATTER present (cover, European foreword, Introduction, §1 Scope, §2 Normative references, partial §3 Terms/definitions to 3.1.30); the full technical body §4-§10 + Annexes A-G (~161 pp) is NOT in this 15-page preview. PRIMARY railway-cybersecurity Technical Specification — the rail-sector application of the IEC 62443 series. Paraphrased (front matter): SCOPE — guidance + specifications for managing cybersecurity within the EN 50126-1 RAMS lifecycle across CS/Signalling/Processing, Rolling Stock and Fixed Installations domains; ensures RAMS characteristics are not compromised by intentional attacks; risk models based on IEC 62443, consistent with IEC 62443-2-1 + ISO 27001/27002. Normative refs: EN 50126-1:2017 (RAMS), EN IEC 62443-3-2:2020 (security risk assessment for system design), EN IEC 62443-3-3:2019 (system security requirements + Security Levels), IEC 62443-2-1:2010 (IACS security program). STRUCTURE (ToC): railway system overview (asset model, physical-architecture model, high-level ZONE model); cybersecurity within the railway application lifecycle; system definition + initial risk assessment (ZONES & CONDUITS breakdown); detailed risk assessment (SL-T derivation, countermeasures from 62443-3-3); cybersecurity requirements (system security requirements + foundational classes); cybersecurity ASSURANCE + system acceptance (cybersecurity case, verification, validation, INDEPENDENCE); OPERATION/maintenance/disposal (vulnerability management, SECURITY PATCH MANAGEMENT incl. "vulnerability and outage time during system update, maintenance phase", Fig 15-16); Annexes — handling conduits (A), handling LEGACY systems (B), cybersecurity design principles (C), SAFETY AND SECURITY (D), risk-acceptance methods (E), railway architecture & zoning (F), deliverables (G). Key defs: cybersecurity <railway application> (prevent/detect/react to attack that could lead to accident/unsafe/degradation), zones/conduits, data diode, defence-in-depth | Standard (primary — CENELEC TS via SIST national adoption; iTeh PREVIEW, front matter only) | CENELEC CLC/TS 50701:2021 via SIST-TS CLC/TS 50701:2021, iTeh watermarked preview; file "SIST-TS-CLC-TS-50701-2021.pdf", Download folder | A | R14 (cybersecurity — the railway-cybersecurity standard) + ADR-012 (named sector interface, now on file; §10 security-patch-management = the security-update-as-change thread) (+ PR16 cyber conformance; CSO-validation §3 TS-50701 zone-conduit method; R11/Annex-D safety+security coupling; PR13/PR14/Annex-B legacy) | validate | A by SOURCE — the actual CENELEC railway-cybersecurity Technical Specification (CLC/TS 50701:2021, approved 2021-05-11, CLC/TC 9X), accessed via the Slovenian national adoption (SIST-TS CLC/TS 50701, identical text). CAVEATS: (1) iTeh PREVIEW — ONLY FRONT MATTER present (cover, foreword, Introduction, §1 Scope, §2 refs, partial §3 defs to 3.1.30); the full technical body §4-§10 + Annexes A-G (~161 pp) is NOT in this 15-page file → structure/scope known from ToC, but NO clause-level citation of the technical body possible (same caveat class as the TS 102 281 iTeh preview, E-2026-07-05-04; obtain the full copy for load-bearing clause citation); (2) the real CENELEC 50701 is COMMERCIAL/paywalled (the repo's "CENELEC paywalled/accepted-residual" thread, E-2026-07-06-11), this SIST/iTeh preview is the accessible proxy; (3) 2021 edition (verify whether a later CLC/TS 50701 revision exists). COMPLETES THE SECURITY-HALF of the FRMCS shortlist: TS 33.501 (E-2026-07-26-14) = 5G-generic security · THIS = the RAILWAY cybersecurity standard — the two security anchors ADR-012 rests on. GROUNDS ADR-012 (CRA/NIS-2 secure-by-design, Proposed) with the sector-cybersecurity standard it already NAMES as a sector interface (Legal-basis line) — now the actual document is on file (partial). Directly on-thread for ADR-012's CORE PRINCIPLE: §10.3 SECURITY PATCH MANAGEMENT + Fig 15-16 "vulnerability and outage time during system update (maintenance phase)" IS the "security updates are changes, govern under change-control at the maintenance window" point (ADR-012 Decision §2, the expedited-but-gated path); and Annex D "Safety and security" is the "security ≠ safety but they couple" framing (ADR-012 Context). VALIDATES R14 (cybersecurity conformance, Open) + PR16 (cyber non-conformance) with the primary railway-cybersecurity TS. VALIDATES the CSO-VALIDATION §3 (cso-validation-2026-07-21.md) TS-50701/62443 ZONE-AND-CONDUIT method — §6.4 zones/conduits breakdown + Annex F railway zoning are exactly the zone-conduit artifact the CSO review recommended (the Z1-Z6 partition). Also: Annex B "handling LEGACY systems" = PR13/PR14 (EoL/legacy security) kin; §9.5 INDEPENDENCE in cybersecurity acceptance = the ISA/NoBo independence thread (R11/ADR-004 kin). DOES NOT flip a status: primary grounding of R14/ADR-012 + validation of the CSO-validation method, but preview-only + ADR-012 Proposed + R14 Open — no matrix/ADR change. REFERENCE-TREE: identifies the IEC 62443 series as its normative basis (EN IEC 62443-3-2/-3-3, IEC 62443-2-1) + EN 50126-1 (RAMS) — none on file (62443 paywalled, EN 5012x paywalled-residual E-2026-07-06-11). KIN: E-2026-07-26-14 (TS 33.501 — the 5G-security half), ADR-012 (names this sector interface), cso-validation-2026-07-21.md §3 (zone-conduit method this specifies), E-2026-07-05-04 (TS 102 281 — same iTeh-preview caveat), E-2026-07-06-11 (CENELEC-paywalled thread), E-2026-07-24-01 (CCS'25 attack the zone-conduit boundary controls), R14/PR16/ADR-012 |
| E-2026-07-26-16 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 123 280 V18.11.0 (2025-01) = 3GPP TS 23.280 Release 18 — "Common functional architecture to support mission critical services; Stage 2" (published ETSI/3GPP TS; ref RTS/TSGS-0623280vIB0; 333 pp). PRIMARY normative spec for the COMMON Mission-Critical (MC) functional architecture — the shared foundation beneath ALL MC services (MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo), on which the FRMCS Service Stratum is built. Paraphrased scope (title + domain-level, NOT clause-verified — 333 pp, front matter only): the common functional model + architecture for MC services — MC service server, MC service client, common services core; IDENTITY management (IdM), GROUP management (GMS), CONFIGURATION management (CMS), KEY management (KMS), MC service user profile, LOCATION management; on-network + off-network operation; service continuity; migration + interconnection (incl. partner MC systems); FUNCTIONAL ALIAS management; MBMS/MBS media distribution. CAVEATS: (1) GENERIC MC (LTE/5G), not rail-specific — but the FRMCS SERVICE STRATUM IS "centred on the 3GPP MCX framework" (TS 103 764, E-2026-07-26-11), so TS 23.280 applies by INHERITANCE (same framing as TS 33.501); (2) 333 pp — characterized at SCOPE level from title + front matter + domain knowledge, NOT clause-verified (paraphrase, no clause-level citation); (3) Rel-18 — current but MC evolves per release. VALIDATES R5 (digital-rail / MCX service layer, Accepted · ADR-001): this is the COMMON functional architecture (MC service server/client + common services core; IdM/GMS/CMS/KMS/location) beneath ALL MC services — the substrate of the FRMCS Service Stratum, one architectural layer below the service-specific MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo specs (TS 23.379/282/281). STRENGTHENS the PR15 substrate (MCX feature-equivalence, High): the GSM-R feature-parity items ride ON this common architecture — GROUP management (VGCS/VBS-equivalent substrate), FUNCTIONAL ALIAS management (the GSM-R functional-number equivalent that TS 103 792's identity mapping bridges, E-2026-07-26-13), KEY management/KMS, user-profile/config — the 3GPP foundation the MCX-equivalence bar is realized on; but (generic-MC, not the standalone equivalence PROOF) does NOT change PR15's High status. BEARING: R2 (a normative ref of the interworking spec TS 103 792 E-2026-07-26-13 + trackside spec TS 103 765-4 E-2026-07-26-12); R14 (KMS — MC security TS 33.180 builds on this, ADR-012 kin). DOES NOT flip a status: primary MC-architecture grounding of already-Accepted R5 + the High-treated PR15 (R5 Accepted, PR15 High, R2 Accepted, ADR-001 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. REFERENCE-TREE: an MCX-architecture-set item + a normative ref of TS 103 765-4/792; service-specific MCX specs = TS 23.379 (MCPTT, E-2026-07-26-17), TS 23.282 (MCData, E-2026-07-26-18), TS 23.281 (MCVideo, E-2026-07-26-19), TS 33.180 (MC security, E-2026-07-26-20). KIN: E-2026-07-26-11 (TS 103 764 — FRMCS Service Stratum = MCX framework this founds), E-2026-07-26-12/-13 (reference this), E-2026-07-26-14 (TS 33.501 — the 5G-security layer), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS §15 MCX), PR15/R5/ADR-001 |
| E-2026-07-26-17 | 2026-07-26 | ETSI TS 123 379 V18.9.0 (2024-04) = 3GPP TS 23.379 Release 18 — "Functional architecture and information flows to support Mission Critical Push To Talk (MCPTT); Stage 2" (published ETSI/3GPP TS; ref RTS/TSGS-0623379vi90; 299 pp). PRIMARY normative spec for the MCPTT (Mission-Critical Push-To-Talk) functional architecture — the 3GPP mission-critical VOICE service, the direct MCX successor to GSM-R operational voice, built on the common MC architecture (TS 23.280, E-2026-07-26-16). Service-specific spec sitting ON the common architecture TS 23.280 [E-2026-07-26-16]; MCData = TS 23.282, MCVideo = TS 23.281 | Standard (primary — ETSI/3GPP TS; MCPTT voice architecture, published, current) | ETSI TS 123 379 V18.9.0 (2024-04) = 3GPP TS 23.379 Rel-18, RTS/TSGS-0623379vi90 (etsi.org); file "ts_123379v180900p.pdf", Download folder | A | PR15 (MCX feature-equivalence — MCPTT is the VOICE-equivalence spec: group/emergency calls, floor control, pre-emption) + R5 (MCX voice service layer) (+ R2 referenced by the interworking spec TS 103 792; R14 MC voice security) | validate | A — PRIMARY, PUBLISHED, CURRENT normative standard: the foundational 3GPP/ETSI MCPTT (mission-critical VOICE) functional-architecture spec (TS 23.379 Rel-18 V18.9.0, 2024-04) — same source class as TS 23.280 (E-2026-07-26-16) and the FRMCS ETSI specs. CAVEATS: (1) GENERIC MCPTT (LTE/5G), not rail-specific — but FRMCS operational VOICE IS MCPTT (FRMCS SRS §15 + ETSI FRMCS specs; the voice MCX client tight-coupled in the cab radio per ADR-001), so applies by INHERITANCE; (2) 299 pp — SCOPE-level from title + front matter + domain knowledge, NOT clause-verified; (3) Rel-18 — current but MCPTT evolves per release (rail affiliation/functional-alias items land progressively, cf. E-2026-07-01-10). THE single most PR15-RELEVANT SERVICE SPEC: MCPTT is the normative MCX definition of the VOICE features that must reproduce GSM-R's SAFETY-CRITICAL voice — MCPTT group/broadcast calls (VGCS/VBS-equivalent), MCPTT EMERGENCY + imminent-peril (Railway Emergency Call / Notruf-equivalent), FLOOR CONTROL (talker/uplink-control-equivalent), pre-emptive priority (eMLPP-equivalent). It is the equivalence-TARGET spec behind PR15 (MCX feature-equivalence, High): the GSM-R↔MCPTT interworking of TS 103 792 (E-2026-07-26-13) MAPS GSM-R features onto THIS spec's procedures. VALIDATES R5 (MCX service layer, Accepted · ADR-001) at the voice-service level + STRENGTHENS the PR15 substrate — equivalence bar now anchored to the normative MCPTT spec. BUT does NOT change PR15's High status: generic-MCPTT, not the rail-equivalence PROOF — the ADR-007(e) parity regression against the EIRENE (MI) voice bar (FRS 8.1.0/SRS 16.1.0, E-2026-07-02-30/-31) is still the test; rail-specific items land progressively per release (E-2026-07-01-10). BEARING: R2 (normative ref of TS 103 792, E-2026-07-26-13); R14 (MC voice security via TS 33.180, E-2026-07-26-20). DOES NOT flip a status (R5 Accepted, PR15 High, R2 Accepted, ADR-001 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-26-16 (TS 23.280 common architecture this sits on), E-2026-07-26-13 (TS 103 792 maps GSM-R↔MCPTT onto this), E-2026-07-01-10 (named MCX voice gaps), E-2026-07-02-30/-31 (EIRENE MI voice bar), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS §15 MCX), PR15/ADR-007(e)/R5/ADR-001 |

PRIMARY normative spec for the MCData (Mission-Critical DATA) functional architecture — the 3GPP mission-critical DATA service, built on the common MC architecture (TS 23.280, E-2026-07-26-16). Paraphrased scope (title + domain-level, NOT clause-verified — 279 pp, front matter only): MCData functional model + information flows for — SHORT DATA SERVICE (SDS; the GSM-R SDS/text-messaging equivalent), FILE DISTRIBUTION (FD), DATA STREAMING, IP CONNECTIVITY (transporting application data, e.g. ETCS/ATO signalling), transmission/reception control, on-network + off-network operation. R5-CENTRAL — the DATA-signalling bearer: MCData carries the SAFETY-SIGNALLING application data (ETCS Movement Authorities, ATO), so it is arguably more R5-safety-load-bearing than the voice MCPTT — VALIDATES R5 (digital-rail: ATO/ETCS-L2/3 over packet-native bearer + MCX, Accepted · ADR-001) at the data-service level (the packet path ETCS/ATO ride over FRMCS). DOES NOT flip a status: primary MCData grounding of already-Accepted R5/R6 + the High-treated PR15 (R5/R6 Accepted, PR15 High, R2 Accepted, ADR-001 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-26-16 (TS 23.280 common architecture), E-2026-07-26-17 (TS 23.379 MCPTT — sibling), E-2026-07-26-13 (TS 103 792 — SDS↔MCData §9), E-2026-07-26-06 (Kontron SMS↔MCData IWF), E-2026-07-25-09 (5GRAIL — ATO/ETCS over MCData lab test), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS §15 MCX), PR15/R5/R6/ADR-001 |

VALIDATES R5 (digital-rail: real-time video as a carriable FRMCS capability, Accepted · ADR-001) at the video-service level — the packet-native FRMCS bearer enables video that GSM-R (CS, ~9.6 kbit/s) never could (a capability STEP-CHANGE, not an equivalence item). COMPLETES the MCX service trio on file: common architecture (TS 23.280) + voice (MCPTT TS 23.379) + data (MCData TS 23.282) + video (THIS). BEARING: PR15 (completeness only — MCVideo has no GSM-R equivalence obligation); R2 (referenced by the trackside/interworking specs); R14 (MC video security via TS 33.180, E-2026-07-26-20). DOES NOT flip a status: primary grounding of already-Accepted R5 (R5 Accepted, PR15 High [unaffected by video], ADR-001 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. KIN: E-2026-07-26-16/-17/-18 (TS 23.280 common + 23.379 MCPTT + 23.282 MCData — the trio this completes), E-2026-07-12-02 (FRMCS SRS §15 MCX), R5/ADR-001 |

PRIMARY normative spec for MC-SERVICE SECURITY — the security layer for the Mission-Critical services (MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo), atop the common MC architecture (TS 23.280, E-2026-07-26-16) and the 5G-core security (TS 33.501, E-2026-07-26-14). Paraphrased scope (title + domain-level, NOT clause-verified — 209 pp, front matter only): MC-service security architecture — KEY MANAGEMENT (KMS / Key Management Server; identity-based SAKKE/MIKEY key distribution for group + private communications), IDENTITY-management security (OpenID Connect / IdMS; MC user authentication + authorization), MEDIA-plane protection (SRTP/SRTCP end-to-end encryption), SIGNALLING-plane protection (XML encryption + signing), inter-domain / interconnection / migration security, MuSiK/multicast keys. CAVEATS: (1) GENERIC MC security (LTE/5G), not rail-specific — but FRMCS's Service Stratum = the 3GPP MCX framework, so its security = TS 33.180 (by INHERITANCE); (2) 209 pp — SCOPE-level from title + front matter + domain knowledge, NOT clause-verified; (3) Rel-18. COMPLETES the MCX standards set on file (common architecture + MCPTT + MCData + MCVideo + SECURITY) AND the three-layer FRMCS security stack: 5G-core security (TS 33.501, E-2026-07-26-14) → MC-service security (THIS) → railway cybersecurity (TS 50701, E-2026-07-26-15). VALIDATES R14 (cybersecurity conformance, Open) + grounds ADR-012 (secure-by-design, Proposed) at the MC-SERVICE security layer: KMS key management, IdMS authentication, media (SRTP) + signalling (XML) encryption — the MCX-application security atop the 5G-transport security. DIRECTLY FILLS a named PR15 GAP: the Kontron/DSD design study flagged FRMCS MCX "E2E encryption/security to be defined, all security functions OPTIONAL" (E-2026-07-01-10) — TS 33.180 is the normative spec that DEFINES that E2E encryption/key management (KMS/SAKKE); so the security-equivalence side of PR15 (MCX feature-equivalence, High) is now normatively specified — BUT (generic-MC-security, not rail-mandated; the "optional" concern is a DEPLOYMENT/procurement matter, i.e. whether operators mandate it — the same optionality lesson as TS 33.501's N2/N4, E-2026-07-26-14 + ADR-012 action-2) it does NOT close PR15 or R14. BEARING: R5/R6 (secured MCX service/gateway); R2 (media encryption at the GSM-R↔FRMCS interworking boundary, TS 103 792 §7.2.7, E-2026-07-26-13). DOES NOT flip a status: primary MC-security grounding of R14/ADR-012 + the PR15 security substrate (R14 Open, PR15 High, ADR-012 Proposed); no matrix/ADR change. REFERENCE-TREE: MCX standards set now COMPLETE on file (architecture + 4 services + security); the security stack complete (TS 33.501 + TS 33.180 + TS 50701). KIN: E-2026-07-26-16..-19 (the MCX architecture + service specs this secures), E-2026-07-26-14 (TS 33.501 — the 5G-transport security beneath), E-2026-07-26-15 (TS 50701 — the railway-cyber layer above), E-2026-07-01-10 (the named MCX-security-optional gap this fills), E-2026-07-26-13 (TS 103 792 interworking media encryption), ADR-012, R14/PR15/PR16 |

| E-2026-07-29-02 | 2026-07-29 | Council of the EU doc 9794/25 (Brussels, 06.06.2025; Interinstitutional File 2025/0036 NLE; 68 pp) — Outcome of Proceedings: COUNCIL RECOMMENDATION on an EU blueprint for cyber crisis management ("CYBER BLUEPRINT"), approved by Council 06.06.2025; REPLACES Commission Rec (EU) 2017/1584. The Union framework for LARGE-SCALE cybersecurity incidents (disruption exceeding one MS's response capacity OR significant impact on ≥2 MS) and CYBER CRISES (escalated — internal-market/public-security impact), across the full lifecycle: preparedness, SHARED SITUATIONAL AWARENESS, DETECTION, response, recovery. Actors/machinery: EU-CyCLONe (operational, intermediary technical↔political), CSIRTs network (technical), ENISA (secretariat + situational awareness + Union-level exercises), CERT-EU, Commission/EEAS ISAA, IPCR as the political layer; definitions based on NIS-2 (Dir (EU) 2022/2555); CRA (Reg (EU) 2024/2847) in the reporting chain; keeps 2017/1584's three-level approach (technical/operational/political) + subsidiarity. Coherence duty with the EU CRITICAL INFRASTRUCTURE Blueprint (Council Rec 2024/4371 — NON-cyber physical resilience). KEY FINDING (Annex II, sectoral crisis mechanisms for NIS-2 high-criticality sectors): Air → EACCC; Maritime → SafeSeaNet/EMSA/IMS/LRIT; Electricity/Oil/Gas → coordination groups; RAIL → "n/a" — NO Union-level rail sectoral cyber-crisis mechanism exists; only the horizontal Network of Transport Contact Points (Contingency Plan for Transport, COM(2022) 211). A THINK-TANK ANALYTICAL brief (named authors, footnoted) surveying physical + cyber threats to rail. Content: (a) opens on Bad Aibling (09.02.2016, 10 killed) — SPECULATES "one of the safety systems had been disarmed as part of a deliberate act", written BEFORE the cause was published — SUPERSEDED: confirmed cause was DISPATCHER ERROR/distraction (manual override, NOT a cyberattack), cf. E-2026-07-29-01; do NOT cite ICT's Bad-Aibling "deliberate act/cyber" framing; (b) physical-terrorism history (Madrid 2004, London 2005, Ankara 2015, Brussels 2016 — out of scope); (c) Ukraine Dec 2015 BlackEnergy/KillDisk — TrendMicro reported traces in a major Ukrainian railway operator's systems; (d) CYBER CORE = a SUMMARY of the "StrangeLove SCADA" / "Great Train Cyber Robbery" research (Alexander Timorin & Sergei Gordeychik, 3-yr study, presented 2015/32C3): weaknesses in signalling + control (EuroStar 7 automation systems — unauthorized remote access, hard-coded passwords, no auth in old protocols; Siemens Sibas / SIMATIC WinAC RTX PLC; Computer-Based Interlocking (CBI) route-setting) → attackers could trigger shutdown or cause a collision via physical access / insider / phishing / USB. KEY GSM-R FINDING (relayed from StrangeLove): GSM-R SIM cards manage ops + commands incl. the STOP command; trains stop AUTOMATICALLY when the SIM↔central-control link is lost, INCLUDING under GSM jamming (fail-safe); default SIM codes often never changed; modems allow OTA firmware upgrades → hijackable; some GSM-R modems susceptible to mobile-modem attacks. Damian Dudek [VDE ITG], Prof. Dr. Klaus Mößner [TU Chemnitz], Prof. Dr.-Ing. Ralf Tönjes [HS Osnabrück], Dr. Matthias Wirth [VDE ITG]). An INDEPENDENT expert-society briefing taking the 23 Jun 2026 DB outage as its explicit occasion, comparing GSM-R vs FRMCS RESILIENCE — remarkably aligned with this engagement's thesis. HEADLINE (independent corroboration of the SILENT-FAULT-FAILOVER finding): per DB ("Laut Auskunft der Bahn"), the 23 Jun cause was the swap of a SWITCH (network-distribution component) producing a software error — "this would normally not have been a problem, since the systems are redundant and would have switched to the parallel system, BUT the switchover signal FAILED TO OCCUR ('das Umschaltsignal blieb aus') and it had to be MANUALLY checked and switched" — i.e. redundancy existed but the FAILOVER TRIGGER did not fire (the exact silent-fault/failover-trigger-gap the engagement identified: finding-failover-trigger-flow-gap.md, silent-fault-failover-cascade-sequence.md, ADR-007, ADR-012 decision-point-5). TECHNICAL COMPARISON: GSM-R = 2G/GSM-900 (1991-vintage), exclusive 876–880/921–925 MHz, 200 kHz fixed channel, circuit-switched, hierarchical/CENTRALIZED ("a fault in the GSM-R core can cause widespread outage"); no MIMO/adaptive/SBA/slicing. FRMCS = 5G-SA, Service-Based Architecture (microservices over a logical bus, HTTP/2-3+JSON/REST), STATELESS (session data in HA databases → if a control instance crashes another takes over seamlessly), cloud-native, exclusive frequencies + two-band redundancy, deterministic latency, hard QoS, SIL-4-certifiable. Notes ETCS movement authorities severed instantly on a GSM-R hardware failure (session+logic coupled) vs FRMCS seamless continuity. TIMELINE relayed: MORANE 2 validating spec v2/v3 → FRMCS v3 "1st Edition" freeze end-2027; EU legal framework ~2028; DB rollout 2026–2028 test fields (parallel GSM-R+FRMCS) → 2028–2032 main corridors → 2032–2035 GSM-R shutdown + full FRMCS. BALANCED CAVEAT: public 5G (~95% area, ~23,500 white spots) is technically superior BUT NOT a substitute — lacks rail functionality + redundant all-line coverage. A METHODOLOGY paper: extends FMEA (Failure Mode & Effects Analysis; Severity–Occurrence–Detectability → RPN) from reliability into CYBER-induced failure modes (data integrity/availability/authenticity) for railway IoT/OT, coupled with STREAMING SENSOR TELEMETRY so risk indicators evolve dynamically ("rather than remain static documentation artefacts") — explicitly to operationalize NIS2 ARTICLE 21 (risk-management) via field-layer evidence. Demonstrated on a scenario-based dataset from ~250 trackside/rolling-stock/environmental/power sensors over a 25 km segment, with controlled SPOOFING/REPLAY/INJECTION anomalies; KPIs = detection latency, MTTD (Mean Time To Detect), post-mitigation RPN contraction; "reduce undetected exposure windows". Three contributions: (1) FMEA extended to cyber failure modes; (2) real-time anomaly indicators → dynamic RPN updates; (3) FMEA→NIS2 Art 21 mapping evidenced via data-driven KPIs. NOTABLE ACK (relevant to ADR-011): "safety certification cycles restrict frequent software changes" — the same change-control-under-safety-case tension the engagement flags. A meta-level SYSTEMATIC LITERATURE REVIEW of ACADEMIC RESEARCH addressing the NIS2 Directive (Dir (EU) 2022/2555): notes NIS2 enlarges the critical-infrastructure scope beyond the old (energy/transport-only) 2016 NIS directive to 11 high-criticality sectors (Annex I — incl. TRANSPORT — + Annex II other critical sectors); essential vs important entities by "criticality" (Art 2); strengthened CSIRTs + public↔private coordination. FINDS: research typically frames NIS2 alongside the EU's other cyber laws (CRA etc.); operates across ICS, telecom, energy/water, and INFO-SHARING / SITUATIONAL-AWARENESS infrastructures; flags research GAPS.
