| E-2026-07-30-03 | 2026-07-30 | UIC IRS 90940 Ed. 3 (International Railway Solution), "SFERA Protocol: Train Operation Data Exchange with On-Board Devices" (© UIC Paris 2026; ISBN 978-2-7461-3545-1; approbation 15/10/2025, effective 27/02/2026; 103 pp; UIC Chapter 9 "Digitalisation, Data, Emerging Innovations", Subsection 4). UIC NORMATIVE application-protocol standard for SFERA — the ground↔on-board data exchange for DRIVER ADVISORY SYSTEMS (DAS): Standalone (S-DAS) + Connected (C-DAS), advised speeds, timetable mode, ATO-over-ETCS, TMS integration, EoA; designed as a SUPERSET of ETCS SUBSET-126; uses the TAF/TAP Common Interface for IM-RU. KEY FACTS: (1) TRANSPORT-AGNOSTIC — "SFERA is compatible with any network connection that supports TCP/IP (e.g. the Internet)"; built on IETF/OASIS protocols — OASIS MQTT v5.0 (broker), WebSocket (RFC 6455), TLS 1.3 (RFC 8446), JWT (RFC 7519), UUID (RFC 4122); (2) APP-LAYER SECURITY BY DESIGN — TLS 1.3 + JWT authentication ("authentication is a prerequisite for access… verifies the legitimacy of a transmission, message, or originator"); cybersecurity-motivated architecture — the IM "prefers not to establish direct connections with RU devices (e.g. for cybersecurity)", using an MQTT broker as intermediary. VALIDATES (concretely, in a SHIPPING standard): R6 — SFERA is THE exemplar of "transport transparent to the application" (the R6/ADR-001 gateway-decoupling principle): explicitly transport-agnostic over TCP/IP → runs UNCHANGED over GSM-R packet today and FRMCS 5G tomorrow; superset of ETCS SUBSET-126. SDO-MAPPING relevance (sdo-mapping-frmcs-gsmr-5gsa.md): SFERA shows the APPLICATION layer rides IETF/OASIS protocols (MQTT/WebSocket/TLS/JWT) — a DIFFERENT SDO stack from the 3GPP/ETSI bearer; a UIC-owned app protocol above the transport, exemplifying stratum decoupling. | E-2026-07-30-09 | 2026-07-30 | UIC Digital Solutions / Rail System Forum — "Artificial Intelligence for Predictive Maintenance (AIPM) — Executive Report Year 1 (2022)" (UIC; March 2023; publication code 5-23008E; 12 pp). UIC working-group (AIPM project) executive report — members incl. UIC (ATOS), ADIF, Irish Rail (CIE), DB Netz, FS/RFI, MAV, ÖBB Infra, SBB, SNCF (European operators/IMs). FOCUS — MAINTENANCE CLASSIFICATION (Figure 1, per EN 13306 "Maintenance terminology"): MAINTENANCE → PREVENTIVE (before failure) + CORRECTIVE (after failure); PREVENTIVE → PREDETERMINED (scheduled/time-or-usage-based) + CONDITION-BASED; CONDITION-BASED → PREDICTIVE. EN 13306 definition of PREDICTIVE MAINTENANCE = "condition-based maintenance carried out following a forecast derived from repeated analysis or known characteristics and evaluation of the significant parameters of the degradation of the item" — the forecast/degradation-driven branch where AI/ML sits ("the next level" beyond time-/condition-based preventive maintenance). REPORT CONTENT: AI analyses large multi-source equipment data → pattern-ID + failure prediction; rail use-cases (point mechanism, Switch & Crossings); links predictive data to Asset Management (AM) + sensor-naming conventions; PoV-before-scale; benefits (asset utilization, safety, cost, efficiency); challenge = cultural change. READ: full 12 pp (exec-level) | Sector report (primary — UIC working-group report; relays EN 13306 taxonomy) | file "5-23008E.pdf", Download folder = UIC AIPM Executive Report Year 1 (2022), March 2023 (© UIC; paraphrased, not quoted — UIC IP) | A | R12 (condition-monitoring / failure-prediction = the DETECTION/awareness / Risk-Sentinel theme; the EN 13306 maintenance taxonomy classifies the monitoring types) + R9 (AI application — MAINTENANCE AI/ML, adjacent to the traffic-oversight AI of ADR-002) + R13 (asset/fleet maintenance — predictive maintenance linked to Asset Management; ties E-2026-07-30-08) + R5 (digital-rail capability — AI predictive maintenance) (+ glossary/data-model: the EN 13306 maintenance classification as a reference taxonomy) | validate | A — PRIMARY UIC OFFICIAL WORKING-GROUP REPORT (UIC Digital Solutions / Rail System Forum; AIPM project; European-operator membership). FOCUS DELIVERED (per request) — the EN 13306 MAINTENANCE CLASSIFICATION (Fig 1): corrective vs preventive; preventive = predetermined (scheduled) + condition-based; condition-based → predictive (forecast/degradation-driven, where AI/ML sits) — a clean REFERENCE TAXONOMY for classifying the Risk-Sentinel's monitoring + the maintenance dimension (candidate glossary/data-model entry). SCOPE: defines the FRMCS SERVICE STRATUM — the communication + complementary service resulting from 3GPP MISSION CRITICAL SERVICES (MCX) + the 3GPP multimedia system (IMS). Determines: (1) building blocks + reference points of 3GPP MCX/IMS; (2) reference points BETWEEN MC systems/IMS for interconnection + unilateral use by MC organizations (MC MIGRATION); (3) reference points between the MC system/IMS and external systems; (4) QoS + priority management; (5) USER IDENTIFICATION, AUTHENTICATION and AUTHORIZATION; (6) COMMUNICATION SECURITY aspects; (7) complementary/auxiliary service (e.g. alternative user addressing schemes = functional alias). THE 3GPP FRMCS USE-CASES / REQUIREMENTS STUDY — the "highest-priority gap" now closed with the actual primary (SUPERSEDES the indirect 3GPP webpage railways1, which fetch-403'd). Based on the UIC FRMCS FUNCTIONAL WORKING GROUP's User Requirements Specification (URS) — the UIC/ETSI TC-RT input translated into 3GPP-style use cases + requirements. INSTRUCTION MISMATCH (on record): the "focus on classifying maintenance, Fig 1, EN 13306" does NOT apply — a 3GPP FRMCS requirements study, NO maintenance/EN 13306 content (grep: 0 hits); template carryover. SCOPE (§1): "FRMCS will adapt 3GPP transport … will resemble GSM-R and additionally provide capabilities beyond GSM-R — higher data rates, lower latency, multimedia, improved reliability; end-to-end use cases; requirements that might/might not be in scope of existing 3GPP specs; interworking requirements GSM→FRMCS for smooth migration." STRUCTURE: three communication categories — clause 6 CRITICAL (incl. §6.4 Railway Emergency Communication: initiation/entry/change/leave/termination of the railway emergency alert, emergency voice + data; §6.8 public emergency; §6.20/6.22 SERVICE INTERWORKING WITH GSM-R), clause 7 PERFORMANCE, (clause 8 BUSINESS) communication use cases. The OT/ICS-cybersecurity ASSET-OWNER (operator) security-program standard — the core of the ISA/IEC 62443 series that CENELEC TS 50701 profiles for rail (E-2026-07-26-15); the "3GPP-role for security" / OT-cyber technology core in the security SDO sub-tree. SOURCING CAVEAT (honesty): the logged PDF is an iTeh.ai catalog PREVIEW (watermarked "PREVIEW"; standards.iteh.ai) — front matter + full ToC read; the NORMATIVE CLAUSE BODIES are paywalled (not in the preview); the doc warns "obtain from an authorized distributor" — verify authorized provenance before load-bearing citation. STRUCTURE (from ToC): Concepts — MATURITY LEVELS (ML) + SECURITY LEVELS (SL) + roles (asset owner / service provider / product supplier); Conformance + assessment (conformity evidence, profiles, asset-owner conformity assessment); then 8 SECURITY PROGRAM ELEMENTS (SPE) — SPE 1 Organizational (ISMS, background checks, roles, awareness, SUPPLY-CHAIN SECURITY, risk mitigation, anomaly discovery, physical access); SPE 2 Configuration mgmt (asset inventory, CHANGE CONTROL); SPE 3 Network/comms security (system segmentation/ZONES, segmentation from SAFETY systems, secure wireless, secure remote access); SPE 4 Component security (hardening, malware, PATCH MANAGEMENT); SPE 5 Data protection (classification, crypto, KEY MANAGEMENT, integrity); SPE 6 User access control (MFA, mutual auth, authorization); SPE 7 Event & incident mgmt (event DETECTION, logging, incident handling, VULNERABILITY HANDLING); SPE 8 System integrity & AVAILABILITY (CONTINUITY mgmt, availability, FAILURE-STATE, backup/restore). SIBLING of A1:2020 (E-2026-07-31-05, Annex ZZ harmonisation) — the amendment pair for the EN 50128:2011 spec is now complete on file. KEY CONTENT (from preview, sections 1–10): (1) GENERAL CHANGES — all "SIL 0" replaced by "BASIC INTEGRITY" (EN 50126-1:2017, 3.7); "safety function(s)" → "safety-RELATED function(s)"; "EN 50126-1" → "EN 50126-1 and EN 50126-2"; "assessment" means INDEPENDENT SAFETY ASSESSMENT (EN 50126-1:2017, 3.33); (2) INTRODUCTION MODIFIED — the SAFETY↔SECURITY SEAM stated in the safety standard's OWN text: EN 50128 "does not specify the requirements for … security policies or security services"; "IT security can affect not only the operation but also the FUNCTIONAL SAFETY of a system. For IT security, appropriate IT security standards should be applied" — NOTE names ISO 27000 series, ISO/IEC TR 19791 and the IEC 62443 SERIES → EN 50128 itself DELEGATES IT security to IEC 62443; (3) 4.4 REPLACED — "At least the Basic Integrity requirements of this European Standard shall be fulfilled for the software part of functions that have a safety impact BELOW SIL 1" (NOTE: Basic Integrity also usable for non-safety-related software); (4) 3.1.17 "PRE-EXISTING SOFTWARE" — explicitly includes "commercial off-the-shelf software, OPEN-SOURCE software and software previously developed but not in accordance with this European Standard"; (5) Clause 2 normative refs replaced (EN 50126-1/-2:2017, EN 50129:2018, EN ISO 9000/9001:2015, ISO/IEC 90003:2014, ISO/IEC 25000 series); scope adds 1.10 FPGA/CPLD → EN 50129:2018 Annex F; definitions rebased on IEC 60050/EN 50126-1:2017 (error/fault/failure, risk, safety, validation, verification, safety-related); Validator-role combination rules (5.1.2.10 n / 5.1.2.11 m); V&V/assessment report restructuring (6.2.4.10/.11 Software Planning Verification Report; removals in 6.3/6.4). SOURCING CAVEAT (honesty): iTeh.ai PREVIEW (watermarked; standards.iteh.ai), SIST adoption identical per cover — and UNLIKE A1 this preview is TRUNCATED: 9 pp on file vs ToC running to p.14 — sections 11–17 (6.5 QA, 6.7 support tools/languages, Clause 7 generic software development, Clause 8 application data/algorithms, Clause 9 SOFTWARE DEPLOYMENT AND MAINTENANCE, ANNEX A Criteria for Selection of Techniques and Measures, Annex C) are NOT in the preview — do NOT cite those changes from this copy. THE SCAS CATALOGUE — the generic security-requirements + TEST-CASE catalogue applied to all 3GPP network product classes (per-product SCAS specs reference it); the PRODUCT-ASSURANCE half of the §4a equipment-assurance pair (3GPP SCAS + GSMA NESAS). GTP-C FILTERING (4.2.6.2.3) + GTP-U FILTERING (4.2.6.2.4); §4.3 HARDENING — no unnecessary services, restricted reachability, no unused software/functions, remote-login restrictions, OS hardening (IP-spoofing mitigation, MINIMIZED KERNEL NETWORK FUNCTIONS, SYN-flood, buffer-overflow protection), web-server hardening, traffic separation, SBA/JSON-parser rules; §4.4 BASIC VULNERABILITY TESTING — port scanning, vulnerability scanning, robustness/FUZZ testing. | E-2026-08-01-03 | 2026-08-01 | ETSI TS 133 210 V17.1.0 (2022-09) = 3GPP TS 33.210 Release 17 — "Network Domain Security (NDS); IP network layer security" (PUBLISHED normative ETSI/3GPP TS; 30 pp; COMPLETE TEXT, free official ETSI publication). THE NETWORK-DOMAIN / INTERCONNECT-SECURITY spec + the CENTRAL CRYPTO-PROFILE REPOSITORY: "the stage-2 specification for IP related security in the 3GPP and fixed broadband core networks"; per its Introduction, born from the GPRS-era ABSENCE of core-network security ("formerly perceived not to be a problem… no longer the case") — and "starting with LTE, but especially with 5G, security of signalling protocols moves onto the application layer. The current document is the central repository of the protection mechanisms and profiles for these protocols". STRUCTURE: §4 SECURITY DOMAINS + SECURITY GATEWAYS (SEGs) — Za (inter-domain, between SEGs of DIFFERENT security domains = the interconnect border) / Zb (intra-domain) interfaces; §5 key mgmt + IPsec profiling (ESP, tunnel mode, IKEv2, SPD/SAD, security-policy granularity); §6 OTHER 3GPP PROFILES — TLS 1.3 (RFC 8446) + TLS 1.2 profiles, JWE/JWS profiles (the primitives beneath SBA/SEPP N32 application-layer security, TS 33.501); ANNEX B (NORMATIVE) — SECURITY PROTECTION FOR GTP: need for protection, POLICY DISCRIMINATION OF GTP-C vs GTP-U, protection of GTP-C transport/interfaces; ANNEX C (normative) — IMS protocol protection (Mm/Mw; FRMCS's session layer); Annex D — UTRAN/GERAN IP transport (legacy side). Title spans GSM (Phase 2+) → UMTS → LTE → 5G = applies across the dual-run generations. | E-2026-08-01-05 | 2026-08-01 | ENISA "Signalling Security in Telecom SS7/Diameter/5G — EU level assessment of the current situation" (March 2018; ISBN 978-92-9204-252-3, DOI 10.2824/38400; © ENISA 2018; 30 pp; COMPLETE TEXT) — produced with the European Commission, Art. 13a Expert Group, provider members of GSMA and ETIS; peer reviewers incl. Holtmanns, Mc Daid. THE EU-INSTITUTIONAL STATEMENT OF THE INTERCONNECT-SIGNALLING PROBLEM — the engagement's §4a finding, stated by the EU cyber agency in 2018: interconnect = a trust-based "WILD WEST" on protocols "designed decades ago" without security (SS7/SIGTRAN; PSTN-inherited trust model; deregulation multiplied interconnected parties incl. non-operators); risk level assessed MEDIUM-TO-HIGH; attacks "based on the exploitation of LEGITIMATE SS7/Diameter traffic/messages making it very difficult to detect" (= the blending mechanism GTPDOOR later exemplified); "most of the security work has to be done at the PROVIDERS' level" (subscribers cannot self-protect); Diameter (4G) kept "the same interconnect principles" and "has been proved vulnerable"; 5G (then in development): "a certain risk of REPEATING HISTORY… having the same security risks could be extremely dangerous". DOCUMENTED ATTACK LIST: GTP data-session hijacking; O2 GERMANY SS7 mTAN interception → bank-account draining (DE precedent); location tracking; subscriber-profile extraction via Diameter; DoS on an operator; Nordic regulators' joint SS7 recommendations (2015). RECOMMENDATIONS: to EC — "consider revising the current LEGAL LANDSCAPE so that signalling security is covered" + baseline security requirements for providers (2018: NOT covered); to NRAs — national analysis + incident-reporting coverage; to industry/SDOs — "ensure security is covered properly within the new 5G standards". | E-2026-08-01-06 | 2026-08-01 | ECC Decision (20)02 (CEPT/ECC; approved 20 November 2020; entry into force 20 Nov 2020, preferred national implementation 20 May 2021; 12 pp; COMPLETE TEXT) — "Harmonised use of the paired frequency bands 874.4-880.0 MHz and 919.4-925.0 MHz and of the unpaired frequency band 1900-1910 MHz for Railway Mobile Radio (RMR)". THE PRIMARY SPECTRUM INSTRUMENT — the exact decision the R7 matrix row cites; closes the last SECONDARY-ONLY layer in the SDO map (spectrum was anchored via VDE/TS 103 793 refs). SCOPE: RMR "encompasses GSM-R and its successor(s), including FRMCS"; supports Dir 2016/797 + CCS TSI (Reg 2016/919); REPLACES/withdraws the GSM-R-era ECC Decisions (02)05, (02)09, (02)10; EC Mandate to CEPT (2018) answered by CEPT Reports 74 + 76; GSM-R legacy bands 876-880/921-925 (1999/569/EC + (02)05) extended to 874.4-880/919.4-925; national extension bands 873-876/918-921 per ECC (19)02. DECIDES: CEPT administrations designate the bands for RMR "on a NON-EXCLUSIVE basis" + allow free circulation/use of RMR terminals; cab-radio defined as RMR terminal supporting voice AND data (e.g. ETCS); Annex 1 GSM-R technical conditions (200 kHz raster, e.i.r.p. masks); Annex 2 wideband RMR LRTC/BEM in 900 MHz (5.6 / 5 / 1.4 MHz + 200 kHz NB-IoT standalone channels; cab-radio ≤31 dBm, ACLR 37 dB, mandatory uplink power control; RECEIVER-ROBUSTNESS/blocking requirements Tables 7-8); Annex 3 wideband RMR 1900-1910 MHz TDD (10 MHz, 65 dBm/10 MHz; receiver blocking Tables 11-12); coexistence provisions: MFCN (enhanced BS selectivity assumption + coordination, ECC Report 229), DECT 1880-1900 worst-case measures, national PPDR coordination in 1900-1910. KEY CONSIDERANDA: (e) "RMR is NOT a safety service (ITU Radio Regulations No. 1.59)"; (j) GSM-R→FRMCS migration "expected from 2024 onwards"; (p) ETSI to develop Harmonised Standards for GSM-R successor(s); footnote 4: wideband technologies = "LTE OR NR, including NB-IoT" (technology-neutral spectrum). | E-2026-08-01-14 | 2026-08-01 | UNISIG SUBSET-147 v1.0.0 (05/07/2023) — "ERTMS Data Applications — FFFIS part: CCS Consist Network Communication Layers" (COMPLETE TEXT, 43 pp; ETCS Baseline 4 Release 1; approved by the ERTMS supplier consortium: Alstom, AZD, CAF, Hitachi Rail STS, Mermec, Siemens, Thales). THE ON-BOARD CCS NETWORK SPEC — mandated in the CCS TSI Table A2 index 90 (Reg (EU) 2026/693, E-2026-08-01-11), v1.0.0. WHAT: defines the network technology for the on-board CCS subsystem — the communication among ETCS on-board, ATO on-board, and the FRMCS GATEWAY, and the interface to rolling stock. Freezes existing tech (MVB §4, CAN §5, legacy SUBSET-119/-121) AND defines the FUTURE HARMONISED platform: the ETHERNET CCS CONSIST NETWORK (ECN per IEC 61375-3-4), OSI layers 1-2 specified + which technologies allowed at layers 3-6 (§8). KEY CONTENT for the engagement: (1) Figure 2-1 puts the FRMCS GATEWAY as a first-class on-board element — SUBSET-147 = the on-board network the FRMCS bearer plugs into via that gateway, ETCS/ATO apps sharing the consist network through VLANs (TCMS / ETCS-ATO / FRMCS instances) = the ON-BOARD realization of "transport transparent to the application" (TOBA/OB_GTW/OBapp, R6); (2) LOGICAL ZONES (§2.4/§8.3.2) — "in alignment with the security concepts the typical zones are: FRMCS, CCS, Rolling Stock, TOS, Passenger Network" + §8.4.3 separation/segmentation + §8.4.7 Security + §8.3.3 Relations to security = the ON-BOARD, train-level analogue of IEC 62443/TS 50701 zones-conduits (FRMCS zone separated from CCS/RST/passenger by construction); (3) §8.4.8 Aspect of Safety = the on-board-network expression of freedom-from-interference-by-segregation (vital CCS zone vs non-vital zones, ADR-004 kin); (4) §9 TRAIN TIME AND LOCATION SERVICE (TTLS) — NTP/PTP/TP (RFC 868) time sync + §9.3.5 Security of TTLS (time-integrity, §3b determinism kin); (5) REQUIREMENT SCOPE: (R) clauses mandatory for newly developed vehicle designs requiring first authorisation + IC ETCS On-board; voluntary for existing designs. References SUBSET-146 (ERTMS E2E Security — "reference to S-146 corrected" in the 1.0.0 change) + SUBSET-139/143 + IEC 61375. | E-2026-08-01-15 | 2026-08-01 | UNISIG SUBSET-146 v4.0.0 (05-07-2023) — "ERTMS End-to-End Security" (COMPLETE TEXT, 41 pp; ETCS Baseline 4 Release 1; approved by the ERTMS supplier consortium: Alstom, AZD, CAF, Hitachi Rail STS, Mermec, Siemens, Thales; © UNISIG). THE MANDATED ERTMS APPLICATION-LAYER SECURITY SPEC — CCS TSI Table A2 index 10d (Reg (EU) 2026/693, E-2026-08-01-11), v4.0.0; the on-board E2E security spec SUBSET-147 references (E-2026-08-01-14). WHAT: interoperable END-TO-END SECURITY for ERTMS data applications (ETCS, ATO, KM = Key Management) over OPEN TRANSMISSION SYSTEMS — the SECURITY LAYER, defined as TLS, sitting DISTINCT FROM the safety layer (Fig 1). CONTENT: §4 Cyber Security Functions — PUBLIC KEY INFRASTRUCTURE (X.509 certs; Root/Intermediate/Issuing CA-RA hierarchy; CRL + OCSP revocation; CMP delivery, RFC-4210/6712), TRANSPORT LAYER SECURITY (TLS 1.3, RFC 8446 + RFC 9150 integrity-only cipher suites; ANSSI TLS guidance), random number generation (RFC 4086), system-wide UTC time service (NTPv4 RFC 5905); §5 REQUIREMENTS — TLS overall + TLS-PKI + TLS-PSK; §5.5 certificate-delivery interface; §5.6 certificate-status-check interface (OCSP/CRL); §5.7 PKI requirements; §5.9 SUPERVISION & DIAGNOSTICS — §5.9.1 FAIL SECURE PRINCIPLE, error reporting/handling. THE LOAD-BEARING PRINCIPLE (§3.1.1.6, paraphrased): "the SEPARATION of safety and security layer is an enabler to keep SECURITY of the communication UPDATED, WITHOUT THE NEED TO RE-APPROVE ANY SAFETY RATING of the application" — the ERTMS-native architectural answer to the CRA-update-vs-safety-case tension (ADR-011); the safety layer (SS-037 Euroradio, for ATP) and the TLS security layer are deliberately decoupled so security patches run on their own cadence. SCOPE (R2 boundary, §3.1.1.11): applicable for PACKET-SWITCHED bearers (FRMCS); NOT applicable for ETCS-over-GPRS (GSM-R legacy keeps the Euroradio SS-037-1 stack). References CLC/TS 50701:2021 Annex C-4 (E-2026-07-26-15) + heavy IETF (TLS/PKI/OCSP/NTP RFCs) + ITU-T X.500/509 directory + SUBSET-137 (Online KM) + SUBSET-148 (ATO transport/security). | E-2026-08-01-17 | 2026-08-01 | oSIST prEN IEC 63452:2025 (SIST, 2025-09-01) = prEN IEC 63452:2025 = IEC 63452 ED1 — "Railway applications – Cybersecurity" (IEC Committee Draft for Vote, doc 9/3232A/CDV; IEC TC 9 "Electrical equipment and systems for railways", secretariat France; circulated 2025-08-08, voting closed 2025-10-17; SUBMITTED FOR CENELEC PARALLEL VOTING; proposed stability date 2028; © IEC 2025; 7-pp SIST preview on file). RESOLVES THE IEC 63452 SUCCESSOR WATCH (carried in sdo-map since v2) — the reported successor to CLC/TS 50701 is CONFIRMED: real, in progress, at CDV (late-draft) stage, dual IEC/EN track. RESTRUCTURES the rail-cyber picture: TS 50701 (current TS, E-2026-07-26-15) → IEC/EN 63452 ED1 (successor, a FULL IEC/EN standard not a TS; ~2028 stability). STRUCTURE (from ToC, preview): role-based (asset owner / integrator / product supplier); §3 railway-system TAXONOMY + terms equivalence; §4 railway system overview (SO-01..04: identify system, high-level system model, high-level ZONE model, shared cybersecurity services); §5 ENTERPRISE cybersecurity programme (CP-01 OT cyber policy/programme, CP-02 info sharing, CP-03 competency, CP-04 inventory, CP-05 SUPPLY CHAIN, CP-06 risk mgmt, CP-07 business continuity, CP-08 data protection); §6 cyber within the railway-application LIFE CYCLE (LC-01 assign Project Cybersecurity Manager, LC-02 plan/tailor/approve cyber mgmt plan, LC-03 manage product suppliers, LC-04 MANAGE INTERACTION WITH SAFETY AND RAM TEAMS) + §6.4 MAPPING TO THE IEC 62278-1 (= EN 50126 RAMS) LIFE CYCLE; §7 RISK ASSESSMENT for system design (ZR-01 identify SUC + security perimeter + access points, ZR-02 initial risk, ZR-03 PARTITIONING INTO ZONES AND CONDUITS, ZR-04 risk comparison, ZR-05 detailed risk assessment [threats/vulnerabilities/code-of-practice/reference-system], ZR-06 cyber requirements spec, ZR-07 asset owner approval); §8 architecture (AA-01-02 "CYBERSECURITY SHALL NOT ADVERSELY IMPACT ESSENTIAL FUNCTIONS", apportionment, compensating countermeasures, traceability); §9 assurance (CA-01 verification/validation + independence of testers + CYBERSECURITY CASE, CA-02 handover). Of interest to IEC TC 65 (IACS — the 62443 committee); supersedes internal drafts 9/3000/CD, 9/3036A/CC. | E-2026-08-01-20 | 2026-08-01 | IEC PT 63452 "progress status" presentation — Serge BENOLIEL (Alstom; PT 63452 Leader), 3rd ERA/ENISA Conference, Lille, 2 October 2024 (IEC-branded deck, 13 pp; © IEC). PROGRESS/STRUCTURE/NIS-2-MAPPING update on IEC 63452 — the most current PT-status account on file; context for the CDV (E-2026-08-01-17). SCOPE (broadened vs 2022): whole IEC/TC9 — railway networks (highspeed/mainline/freight) + metropolitan transport (metros, tramways, trolleybuses, fully-automated transport) + magnetic-levitated systems; adaptation of IEC 62443 to rail, taking TS 50701 as input. PT: growth 65 (2022-07) → 138 (2024-10) experts, 4 continents / 20 countries, 15 subgroups, liaison with UITP & ERA (ERJU & UNIFE under discussion), exchanges with ENISA. CD STATUS: IEC 63452 CD Ed1 issued + submitted to NC comments — "improvement and extension of TS 50701"; 228 pp (half normative / half informative annexes); ~66→62 formal requirements; CDV in preparation. NIS-2 ARTICLE-21 → 63452 CROSSWALK (the PT's tentative mapping for railway duty holders' OT systems): a) risk-analysis/IS-security policies → 5.2/5.3; b) incident handling → 10.4 Incident mgmt + 10.15 Security monitoring; c) business continuity → 5.9; d) supply-chain security → 5.7; e) security in acquisition/dev/maintenance + vuln handling/disclosure → Clause 8 + Clause 6 + Annex C + 10.9 Vuln mgmt/10.10 advisories/10.11 Patch mgmt/10.13 EoL; f) risk-mgmt effectiveness → 5.8 + Clause 7 zoning + 10.6 continuous verification; g) cyber hygiene/training → 5.5/5.6 + Annex H roles; h) crypto → Annex C (62443-3-3 in rail); i) HR/access/asset → 5.6 inventory + 10.3 access mgmt for O&M; j) MFA/secured voice-video-text/emergency comms → Annex C — CAVEAT (slide): excludes state-level obligations; NIS-2 needs national transposition. STRUCTURE (fuller than E-17): §4 railway system overview; §5 enterprise cyber mgmt (5.2-5.9); §6 lifecycle (mapping to IEC 62278-1); §7 zoning & risk assessment; §8 cyber requirements (8.5-8.9); §9 assurance; §10 OPERATIONAL/MAINTENANCE/DISPOSAL (10.3 access O&M · 10.4 incident · 10.5 maintenance · 10.6 continuous verification · 10.7 cyber-case update · 10.9 vuln mgmt · 10.11 PATCH mgmt · 10.12 patch supply-chain · 10.13 EoL & security-update · 10.15 SECURITY MONITORING · 10.16 decommissioning); ANNEXES A-K (A conduit · B LEGACY systems · C design principles/system-req · D SAFETY AND CYBERSECURITY · E risk acceptance · F system/zone models · G deliverable content · H roles/competencies · I O&M operational guidance · J vuln-mgmt operational guidance · K CLOUD SECURITY). TIMELINE (as of Oct 2024): kickoff 2022-07 ✓ · CD 2023-08 ✓ · CDV → End Q4 2024 · FDIS → 2025 · IS → End Q4 2025. VERSION HISTORY (one-row-per-spec): supersedes the earlier "IEC PT 63452" deck (Benoliel, 2nd ERA-ENISA Conf, Lille, 1 Dec 2022; file "03SRIS-1 - IEC - PT 63452.pdf" on disk) — genesis: NWIP 9/2835/RVN approved 05/2022 (23 NCs, 100%), original plan IS by 2025-07. | E-2026-08-01-21 | 2026-08-01 | UIC FRMCS FFFIS-7950 v1.0.0 (12/02/2023) — "Future Railway Mobile Communication System — Form Fit Functional Interface Specification" (COMPLETE TEXT, 59 pp; ISBN 978-2-7461-3120-0; © UIC Paris 2023; developed by the Shift2Rail Consortium + EECT; v1.0.0 = ERA agency-consistency-check + new Annex B "Interoperability requirements in EU"). MANDATED FRMCS INTERFACE SPEC — CCS TSI Table A2 index 92 (FRMCS Baseline 0; verified on the on-file base act p.L222/485, E-2026-08-01-16). THE OBapp/TSapp DECOUPLING INTERFACE — the primary R6 spec: defines the OBapp (interface between On-Board Application(s) and On-Board FRMCS) + TSapp (interface between Trackside Application(s) and FRMCS Core Network) reference points; per its definitions "On-Board FRMCS achieves a DECOUPLING between On-Board Application(s) and transport service" (= transport-transparent-to-application, R6). CONTENT: §5 general principles (OBapp/TSapp interfaces; Tight-Coupled / Loose-Coupled FRMCS service-session modes; logical E2E connectivity); §6 Performance/Availability/Redundancy/SECURITY requirements (OBapp §6.1-6.3, TSapp §6.4-6.6); §7/8 low-layer specs + protocol stacks (IP versions, local IP allocation); §9 OBapp Functional-Services API (event-stream open/close, local registration/deregistration, session start/status/end, auxiliary-function subscribe/notify/query/unsubscribe, incoming session, abnormal cases, dataflows; §9.20 3GPP MCX Services at OBapp; §9.21 QoS mechanism); §10 TSapp API (§10.4 3GPP MCX at TSapp; §10.5 QoS); Annex A ASN.1 notation of OBapp API messages; Annex B Interoperability requirements in EU (ERA). REFERENCES (§3.2) — settles the management-plane question: 3GPP MCX SERVICE specs (TS 22.280 MCCoRe / 23.280 MC-common / 23.281 MCVideo / 23.282 MCData / 23.379 MCPTT / 24.281/282/379 protocol / 33.180 MC-security) + UNISIG SUBSET-147 + UIC FU-7120/FW-AT-7800/TOBA-7510/FIS-7970 + IETF RFC 9113 (HTTP/2) + RFC 8259 (JSON); NO 3GPP SA5 management-plane refs (no TS 28.312/28.530/NWDAF). | E-2026-08-01-23 | 2026-08-01 | ETSI TS 122 261 V15.9.0 (2021-10) = 3GPP TS 22.261 Release 15 — "5G; Service requirements for the 5G system" (COMPLETE TEXT, 53 pp; © ETSI 2021; ref RTS/TSGS-0122261vf90; SA1). THE FOUNDATIONAL 5G SERVICE-REQUIREMENTS SPEC — the ROOT normative reqs for the whole 5G system (UE + NG-RAN + 5GC) that all 5G (FRMCS included) inherits; the base BENEATH the rail-specific 22.289. Fills the one architecture-relevant gap from the E-2026-08-01-22 coverage analysis (22.261 = the missing key spec; FRMCS Phase 5/Rel-19 touches it → rail reqs feed the base 5G reqs). CONTENT: §5 High-level reqs — MIGRATION TO 5G (interworking between 5G systems, legacy-service support, interoperability with legacy 3GPP = the dual-run at the requirements level); §6 Basic capabilities — NETWORK SLICING, diverse mobility mgmt, multiple access technologies, resource efficiency (efficient user-plane §6.5 / control-plane §6.4.2.4), PRIORITY/QoS/POLICY control §6.7, network capability exposure §6.10, context-aware network §6.11, MARKETS REQUIRING MINIMAL SERVICE LEVELS §6.16, EXTREME LONG-RANGE COVERAGE in low-density areas §6.17 (rural/rail-relevant), eV2X §6.20, NG-RAN sharing §6.21, ACCESS IDENTITIES/CATEGORIES §6.22 (priority access); §7 PERFORMANCE — high data rates/traffic densities §7.1, LOW-LATENCY HIGH-RELIABILITY §7.2 (URLLC scenarios/KPIs), high-accuracy positioning §7.3; §8 SECURITY — authentication/authorization/identity mgmt/regulatory/fraud protection; §9 charging; Annexes — latency needs for verticals (A), positioning (B), COMM-SERVICE AVAILABILITY & RELIABILITY (C), URLLC use cases (D — discrete/process automation, electricity distribution, D.5 INTELLIGENT TRANSPORT SYSTEMS infrastructure backhaul), positioning use cases (E). | E-2026-08-01-24 | 2026-08-01 | UIC FRMCS-T (Transition) v1.0 (19 July 2024) — "Future Railway Mobile Communication System — FRMCS-Transition: Technical Guideline for the Application of FRMCS to 4G Networks" (COMPLETE TEXT, 38 pp; ISBN 978-2-7461-3415-7; © UIC Paris 2024; ref FRMCS-T). THE UIC TRANSITION GUIDELINE — a NEW migration path: running the FRMCS SERVICE STRATUM over a 4G/LTE TRANSPORT STRATUM (instead of 5G-SA), as an interim step, incl. over PUBLIC MNO networks. STRATEGIC: inserts a MIDDLE RUNG in the migration model — GSM-R → FRMCS-T (over 4G) → FRMCS (over 5G-SA); §6.3 "Evolving FRMCS-T to FRMCS" defines the on-ramp. CATEGORY: UIC TECHNICAL GUIDELINE / recommendation ("FTR = FRMCS-T Recommended") — NOT a CCS-TSI-mandated spec (not in Table A2); advisory. AUTHORITATIVE ERA/UIC ACCOUNT of FRMCS introduction in the CCS TSI — grounds the migration triangle + resolves the §4 Version↔Baseline crosswalk + identifies EU-institutional stakeholders. KEY FINDING 1 — THE 7-YEAR DECOMMISSIONING RULE (draft CCS TSI §7.3.1.2): GSM-R may only be taken out of operation when (1) ≥7 years after the FRMCS on-board specs are published in the CCS TSI or an ERA Technical Opinion (Appendix A), (2) ≥5-year notification period (RINF/Network Statement, Access Directive 2012/34/EU Art 27), AND (3) FRMCS is in service; the "7-year counter STARTS when the FRMCS on-board IC specs are completed/published — allowing tendering of the complete FRMCS on-board equipment" → the GSM-R switch-off clock is GATED by tender-completeness (Note 9): earliest switch-off = tender-complete-date + 7 years. KEY FINDING 2 — FRMCS VERSION ↔ RMR BASELINE crosswalk (ERA CCS TSI Roadmap slide): FRMCS V1 = CCS TSI 2022 = RMR Baseline 0 (legal anchor); FRMCS V2 = ERA Technical Opinion (enabler for pilots); FRMCS V3 = subsequent CCS TSI = RMR Baseline 1 (start of the 7-year notification) — resolves the sdo-map §4 last-open axis (Edition/Version↔Baseline). KEY FINDING 3 — corroborations: GSM-R (2G) obsolete "between 2035-2040", replaced by FRMCS (5G) [ERA's own window]; RMR = two radio Class A systems (GSM-R + FRMCS, may coexist or independently — dual-run legal basis); GSM-R continues as Class A "without end-date", FRMCS not yet mandatory for new/upgraded lines/vehicles; FRMCS spec set (FRS/SRS/FFFIS/FIS, TOBA FRS, SS-037-1/2/3, SS-146/147/148) all Approved; NEXT CHALLENGES incl. a PUBLIC-(terrestrial)-NETWORKS FEASIBILITY STUDY "required by EIM and CER" (the regulatory origin of FRMCS-T/public-MNO, E-2026-08-01-24), 5G/3GPP reuse + ETSI alignment, test/validation (5GRail, DSD, Europe's Rail), EU trial funding as "trigger for FRMCS market". STAKEHOLDERS named: ERA, UIC, EIM, CER, ETSI, Europe's Rail JU, 5GRail, DSD, European Commission. The BACKWARD-LINEAGE ANCHOR for sdo-map §2 "Service stratum — voice" (MCPTT ← GSM ASCI VGCS, TS 43.068); named in the 3GPP Work Plan (E-2026-08-01-22) as the GSM-R group-call ancestor of MCPTT (VGCS Rel-7/11 → GCSE Rel-12 → MCX Rel-13+). THE GSM-R ASCI BROADCAST-CALL HERITAGE SPEC — VBS (Voice Broadcast Service) = the one-to-many broadcast-call feature of GSM-R (ASCI: VGCS/VBS/eMLPP); the BROADCAST sibling of VGCS (TS 43.068, E-2026-08-01-26). THE FOUNDATIONAL LAYER-3 CORE-NETWORK PROTOCOL — NAS Mobility Management (MM), Call Control (CC), Session Management (SM/GMM); the generic CC/MM/SM signalling spanning GSM→UMTS→LTE→5G. HONEST FRAMING: a foundational/GENERIC spec (all generations), NOT rail-specific — one of the largest 3GPP specs; the bulk is below the engagement's architecture altitude. RAIL RELEVANCE (subset): (1) the CC/MM Layer-3 protocol GSM-R circuit-switched CALL CONTROL runs on (the signalling foundation beneath GSM-R voice); (2) carries eMLPP (enhanced Multi-Level Precedence and Pre-emption) PRIORITY in the CC SETUP — the THIRD GSM-R ASCI feature (alongside VGCS E-26/-27 + VBS E-28), the priority/pre-emption the Railway Emergency Call + priority calls use. CAVEAT: 24.008 CARRIES eMLPP priority but is NOT the eMLPP spec (= TS 43.067/23.067). THE GSM-R ASCI PRIORITY/PRE-EMPTION HERITAGE SPEC — eMLPP (enhanced Multi-Level Precedence and Pre-emption) = the priority-levels + pre-emption feature of GSM-R (the THIRD ASCI feature); this is its Stage-2 definition (TS 24.008 E-2026-08-01-29 only CARRIES the priority in CC signalling). COMPLETES THE GSM-R ASCI TRIO ON FILE: group calls (VGCS, E-2026-08-01-26/-27) + broadcast (VBS, E-2026-08-01-28) + priority/pre-emption (eMLPP, this). Underlies the GSM-R RAILWAY EMERGENCY CALL (REC/Notruf) priority — the highest-precedence call that PRE-EMPTS others — and the priorities on VGCS/VBS calls. CONTENT: §4 main concepts (PRIORITY LEVELS, pre-emption); §5 general architecture; §8 information storage (HLR state-transition model §8.1.1, VLR/MSC/SIM/GCR); §9 identities; §11 functions & information flow (subscription; call set-up — indication of PRIORITY to BSC/RNC/MS for mobile-originated/terminated + VGCS/VBS priority §11.3.3; §11.4 PRE-EMPTION — choice of communication to pre-empt + release procedures; §11.5 pre-emption at handover/relocation; §11.7 call-independent signalling). | E-2026-08-01-31 | 2026-08-01 | MORANE E 10 T 6001 v4.1 (March 2012) — "FFFS for Functional Addressing" (Form Fit Functional Specification; UIC ERTMS/GSM-R Operators Group + GSM-R Industry Group; ACCESS: Public; approved ERTMS/GSM-R ERIG 8 March 2012; 42 pp; COMPLETE TEXT). MANDATED in the CCS TSI Table A2 index 71 (E-2026-08-01-16). THE GSM-R FUNCTIONAL ADDRESSING SPEC — Functional Addressing = the GSM-R feature that lets users be called by their FUNCTIONAL NUMBER (identifying the function/role — "the driver of a certain train" — NOT the physical terminal); guarantees independence of the number from the terminal used to answer; Functional Numbers structured as EIRENE MSISDN numbers (dialable from external PSTN/ISDN). REALIZATION: "FOLLOW ME (FM)" based on USSD + call-forwarding; §4 requirements; §5 realization (mobile-initiated USSD handling; FM REGISTRATION §5.2 / DEREGISTRATION §5.3 / INTERROGATION §5.4 / TERMINAL NOTIFICATION §5.5; HLR/MS storage); §6 usage; §7 error codes. | E-2026-08-01-32 | 2026-08-01 | MORANE F 12 T 6001 v3 (29 January 2007) — "FIS for Location Dependent Addressing" (Functional Interface Specification; UIC ERTMS/GSM-R Operators Group + GSM-R Industry Group; ACCESS: Public; approved ERTMS/GSM-R ERIG 29 Jan 2007; 9 pp; COMPLETE TEXT). MANDATED in the CCS TSI Table A2 index 74 (E-2026-08-01-16). THE GSM-R LOCATION-DEPENDENT ADDRESSING (LDA) FIS — LDA = the GSM-R feature where a user dials a SHORT CODE identifying a FUNCTION (e.g., "primary train controller", "electricity supply controller", "secondary controller") and the call is routed to the controller RESPONSIBLE FOR THE TRAIN'S CURRENT LOCATION/CELL. MECHANISM (CELL-SPECIFIC ROUTING): the MSC reads the Cell Global Identification (CGI) from the COMPLETE LAYER 3 INFORMATION message (§6.1.1) to identify the correct controller, and routes the 4-digit short code carried in SETUP's Called Party BCD Number (§6.1.2) — so a driver reaches "the controller here" without knowing their number. The addressing-family SIBLING of Functional Addressing (E-2026-08-01-31). CONTENT: §5 interface description (mobile-originated call setup via cell-specific routing triggered by short codes, on A + Um interfaces); §6 message functional definition (COMPLETE LAYER 3 INFORMATION with Cell Identification/CGI; SETUP with 4-digit short code as Called Party BCD Number). REFERENCES: MORANE FFFS for LDA (F 10 T 6001, Table A2 idx 73 — the FFFS half, NOT on file), EIRENE SRS v15, EN 301 515, TR 102 281, O-2773 enhanced LDA v5.0, GSM 05.08/08.08, 3GPP TS 24.087 (UUS)/24.008. | E-2026-08-01-37 | 2026-08-01 | Eisenbahningenieur (EI) 10/2025, pp. 40-44 (DVV Media Group) — "AutomatedTrain: Konzepte und Systembausteine für fahrerlose Züge" (concepts + system building blocks for driverless trains; subtitle: functional sequence of a driverless preparation run with reaction to a track obstacle). Authors: Oliver Knitter, Julian Hey, Annette Scheel, Jakob Schultz, Henning Voelz — ALL DB InfraGO AG (systems engineers / system architect). THE PROJECT: "AutomatedTrain" — BMWE-funded (German Federal Ministry for Economic Affairs & Energy); 9-partner cross-industry cooperation; GoA 4 (Grade of Automation 4 = fully driverless); focus = fully automated PREPARATION/STABLING runs (Bereit-/Abstellfahrten) + automatic coupling/uncoupling; results at InnoTrans Sept 2026; prototype on 2 trains after a 1-year integration+test phase. DIRECT ADR-004 IN-DOMAIN CORROBORATION — the safety-relevant/non-safety-relevant SPLIT: paraphrased, "it is very challenging to realize SAFETY-RELEVANT functions with AI/ML (DIN EN 50716 Annex C.3)"; ZONE 1 (SIL1, highest protection — object in the path within emergency-braking distance) = DETECTION ONLY, no classification, "AI is NOT required" for the safety path; ZONES 2/4 (non-safety-relevant — warning-vest / unauthorized-person recognition) = AI/ML IS used precisely BECAUSE non-safety-relevant — exactly the ADR-004 pattern (AI/ML OUT of the safety-relevant path; safety = detection + SIL; AI = non-safety classification only). ZONE CONCEPT (Tab. 1) with SIL: Zone 1 = SIL1 (emergency brake); Zone 2 = Basic Integrity (beside track, moving toward → service brake); Zone 3 = not-safety-relevant (path beyond brake distance); Zone 4 = not-safety-relevant (unauthorized, report to Leitstelle); reactions = acoustic warning / service brake / emergency brake / report to Leitstelle. SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE (Abb. 1): AT-System = Object Detection System (ODS) + Vehicle Data Logger (VDL) + AUTOMATIC PROCESSING MODULE (APM-OB) → ATO-OB + ETCS-OB → Fahrzeugsteuerung; APM-OB = central control element, SIL set INDIVIDUALLY PER APPLICATION (not the full SIL-4 of ETCS-OB). HUMAN-IN-COMMAND: departure only after Freigabe durch Fahrdienstleiter (dispatcher clearance). STANDARDIZATION: CENELEC-conform GoA-4 specification documents as basis for future European standardization + Zulassung; modular architecture. References DIN EN 50716/50128/50129/50126/50657, DIN EN 15227, TSI TAF/TAP, DSD KI-MELZS. VALIDATES + strengthens the ADR-004/ADR-002 design basis, does not flip: ADR-004 (Proposed — load-bearing) — a LIVE DB InfraGO GoA-4 project independently adopts the exact SIL-4-boundary pattern ADR-004 designed: AI/ML kept OUT of the safety-relevant path (Zone 1 = detection-only + SIL, no AI), AI/ML confined to non-safety-relevant classification (Zones 2/4) — the strongest in-domain corroboration yet of "keep the certifiable path small; the learning components sit outside the vital path" (kin CTMS "not safety-critical" E-2026-07-02-14, SIL4-Cloud E-2026-07-02-03, Cloud4Rail E-2026-07-02-27); grounds DIN EN 50716 Annex C.3 (the AI-for-safety-functions annex, E-2026-08-01-10/-11); R9 (Proposed) — GoA 4 = the full-autonomy end of the ladder (ADR-008); ADR-002 (Proposed) — the AutomatedTrain architecture is the in-domain automation-layer reference, though NB GoA-4 CONTROL/actuation not oversight — the engagement's layer is oversight-not-control, so a DESIGN-BASIS analogue for the safety boundary NOT a model for the oversight layer's authority; R10 (Proposed) — departure-after-dispatcher-clearance = human-in-command; R5/R4 — GoA-4 ATO + obstacle-reaction fail-soft. DOES NOT flip a status: ADR-004/ADR-002/R9/R10 Proposed, R5 Accepted, R4 Strengthened-open; no matrix/ADR change (strengthens the ADR-004 design basis; C-tier corroboration, not the engagement's own FFI evidence). NOTABLE HONESTY: AutomatedTrain is GoA-4 CONTROL (the AI drives within the SIL boundary), distinct from the engagement's oversight-only layer — cite for the AI-outside-the-vital-path boundary principle, NOT as precedent for autonomous actuation. NOT a security item (automation/safety) → master log only. FOLLOW-UP: InnoTrans Sept-2026 AutomatedTrain results (watch); DIN EN 50716 Annex C.3 (AI-for-safety) at assurance phase (ADR-004 action 4). | E-2026-08-01-38 | 2026-08-01 | Deine Bahn 11/2024, pp. 18-23 (Bahn Fachverlag GmbH) — "Projekt AutomatedTrain: Betriebskonzept und Systemarchitektur" (subtitle: fully automated preparation/stabling of trainsets). Authors: Oliver Knitter (Subsystem Architect), Thomas Höhne (Product Owner Systemarchitektur), Jakob Schultz (Project System Architect) — all Digitale Schiene Deutschland, DB InfraGO AG. THE EARLIER COMPANION to E-2026-08-01-37 (cited there as ref [2]) — the OPERATIONAL-CONCEPT + SYSTEM-ARCHITECTURE view of the AutomatedTrain GoA-4 project (E-37 = building-blocks + zone-SIL detail). DISTINCT CONTENT: (1) THE 9-PARTNER CONSORTIUM (named): Bosch Engineering, Codewerk, DB Regio, duagon Germany, IAV, ITK Engineering, Red Hat, Siemens Mobility, TU Dresden — led by DB InfraGO; funded by BMWK (2024 name = Bundesministerium für Wirtschaft und Klimaschutz; renamed BMWE by E-37's 2025 vintage — SAME ministry, renamed); (2) REFERENCE ROUTES: Stuttgart S2 (Filderstadt–Hbf) + Munich S3 (Mammendorf–Ostbahnhof); stabling Stuttgart-Vaihingen + München-Steinhausen; TRAINS: Siemens Mireo (ETCS) + Bombardier/Alstom BR430 (S-Bahn Stuttgart, GoA2 "Digitaler Knoten Stuttgart" retrofit → GoA4); (3) BOUNDARY CONDITIONS: ETCS Level 2 ONLY (no signals), ETCS SV 2.2+ incl. ATO, modes Full Supervision / Automated Driving; min 2-min headway; no coupling/reverse/shunting; NO PASSENGERS except authorized rail personnel; ≤40 km/h; no conductor rails/level crossings; (4) THE 6-STEP OPERATIONAL CONCEPT: 1 auto-Aufrüsten in stabling; 2 auto-run to platform (≤40km/h, ETCS-Fahrerlaubnis, obstacle detection, rain/snow/fog speed reduction, DIN EN 16186-1 driver-field-of-view basis); 3 ÜBERNAHME DURCH TF am Bahnsteig (driver TAKES OVER for the passenger service); 4 Übergabe to auto; 5 auto-run to stabling; 6 auto-Abrüsten — the automated part is ONLY prep/stabling (no passengers); (5) SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE (three central elements, Abb. 3): APM-OB (central control, comms to infrastructure + ETCS/ATO/vehicle-control, decides reactions to ODS objects), ODS (AI + sensors + digital map, object detect/identify), VDL (→ Data Factory/DSD for evaluation + TRAINING FUTURE SYSTEMS); (6) STANDARDIZATION BASIS: architecture built on CENELEC + TSI CCS (ERTMS) + ATO + the SHIFT2RAIL GoA3/4 WORKING GROUP results + DSD generic system architecture; CENELEC Phases 1-5; modular for GoA2→GoA4 upgrade. References DIN EN 50126 (RAMS), DIN EN 16186-1 (driver field of view), DSD Data Factory + Pan-European Railway Data Factory. | E-2026-08-01-39 | 2026-08-01 | ICOIN 2024 (IEEE International Conference on Information Networking) — "An Intent-Driven Management Automation for 5G Mobile Networks" (Yoseop Ahn & Jaehoon (Paul) Jeong, Dept. of Computer Science & Engineering, Sungkyunkwan University, Republic of Korea; © 2024 IEEE; 979-8-3503-3094-6/24; 6 pp; "based on our early IETF Internet Draft" — Jeong is an IETF/I2NSF-active researcher). PEER-REVIEWED ACADEMIC RESEARCH on INTENT-DRIVEN 5G NETWORK MANAGEMENT — an IBN-based Network Management System (NMS) uniting NWDAF + Intent-Based Networking with ML/AI; the RESEARCH-LAYER realization of the sdo-map §1b intent-driven-management pipeline (IRTF/IETF IBN research → 3GPP TS 28.312 "intent document" + NWDAF [TS 23.288/29.520] normative → this). COMPONENTS: (1) NETWORK INTENT TRANSLATOR (NIT) — translates high-level network INTENT → low-level network POLICY (IBN Consumer-Facing Interface ↔ NF-Facing Interface, data-model mapper, NLP for intent translation); (2) CLOSED-LOOP NETWORK CONTROL (CLCS) — dynamically refines the network policy from NF monitoring data; (3) NETWORK AUDIT SYSTEM (NAS) — REMOTE ATTESTATION so all IBN framework elements report their activities, to DETECT SUPPLY-CHAIN + INSIDER attacks (four objectives: verify mgmt system/policy; validate supply-chain/insider risks; assess operational controls; offer corrective actions; audit integrity via remote evidence / blockchain). The IBN Analyzer FUNCTIONS AS NWDAF (TS 23.288/29.520). Built on IETF I2NSF (Interface to Network Security Functions), YANG/YAML, RESTCONF/NETCONF/REST-API per 3GPP; intent constructed per the 3GPP intent document (TS 28.312). Use case: IoT-device data aggregation in 5G. READ: pp 1-4 (abstract, intro, §II terminology, §III NMS, §IV NIT, §V NAS) | Academic research (primary — peer-reviewed IEEE conference paper; single-team proposed framework; NOT a standard) | file "ICOIN2024-An-Intent-Driven-Management-Automation-for-5G.pdf", Download folder = ICOIN 2024, Ahn & Jeong (SKKU), IEEE | A | ADR-002/R9 (agentic oversight — the intent-driven + closed-loop + NWDAF-analytics-verify pattern = the oversight-layer pattern) + R12 (Risk-Sentinel — the Network Audit System + closed-loop monitoring + NWDAF-analyzer = the assurance/detection layer) + R14/ADR-012 (§V securing the intent-driven/agentic layer vs INSIDER + SUPPLY-CHAIN attacks via remote attestation) + R8/PR13 (supply-chain audit) | validate | A — PEER-REVIEWED IEEE ACADEMIC RESEARCH (same class as ACM CCS '25 E-2026-07-24-01). CAVEATS: (1) a SINGLE-TEAM PROPOSED framework (not deployed/normative) — the authors' NMS design, mark as research/proposal; (2) GENERIC 5G, not rail — the FRMCS link is INFERRED (intent-driven management is out of FRMCS normative scope per FFFIS-7950's reference list E-2026-08-01-21 / sdo-map §1b); (3) authors are IETF-active (Jeong/I2NSF) — the paper IS an instance of the §1b research layer, not an independent corroboration of it. VALIDATES + grounds, does not flip: ADR-002/R9 (Proposed) — the paper's intent-driven + closed-loop + NWDAF-verify architecture is a concrete academic realization of exactly the agentic-oversight pattern (declare intent → automation realizes within the bearer → analytics verify/optimize against the intent) that sdo-map §1b maps to ADR-002 (via IRTF RFC 9315 → 3GPP TS 28.312/NWDAF); GUARDRAIL INTACT — the engagement's layer is OVERSIGHT not control, whereas this NMS actively drives NFs (closed-loop control), so it is a DESIGN-BASIS analogue for the intent/assurance pattern, NOT a model for granting the oversight layer actuation; R12 (Proposed) — the Network Audit System + NWDAF-analyzer + closed-loop monitoring = the assurance/Risk-Sentinel role (analytics verify intent = the assurance half); R14/ADR-012 (Open · Proposed) — the NAS's remote-attestation detection of INSIDER + SUPPLY-CHAIN attacks against the management layer is a concrete mechanism for SECURING an autonomous/intent-driven layer (kin the GSMA §6 agentic-AI security E-2026-08-01-36; and R8/PR13 supply-chain); R8/PR13 — supply-chain-attack audit. DOES NOT flip a status: ADR-002/R9/R12 Proposed, R14 Open, R8 open; no matrix/ADR change (grounds the §1b research thread + the ADR-002 intent-driven/assurance pattern + the securing-the-agentic-layer dimension; academic proposal, FRMCS-link inferred). SECURITY item (§V NAS) → also indexed in evidence-log-security.md; defensive/architecture, no attack vector. FOLLOW-UP: (1) fold the intent-driven + NWDAF-verify + Network-Audit-System pattern into ADR-002 as a design reference for the assurance/audit layer (guardrail intact); (2) the 3GPP TS 28.312 (intent-driven management) + TS 28.530 (M&O) normative specs (the §1b candidate fetches — the paper cites the "3GPP intent document"). KIN: sdo-map §1b (the IBN → TS 28.312/NWDAF → ADR-002 pipeline this instantiates), E-2026-08-01-36 (GSMA §6 agentic-AI security — securing the agentic layer), E-2026-08-01-21 (FFFIS-7950 — confirms the management plane is out of FRMCS normative scope, the "inferred" basis), E-2026-07-24-01 (ACM CCS '25 — peer-reviewed 5G security research precedent), ADR-002/ADR-012, R9/R12/R14/R8 |
| E-2026-08-02-01 | 2026-08-02 | ER-ISAC presentation "European Rail Information Sharing and Analysis Center (ER-ISAC)" — Olivier DE VISSCHER (ER-ISAC Chairman, Belgium), with Thomas Faenoe (CIO, Rail Net Denmark) + contribution from Dr Jasmin Cosic (DB Netze Germany); delivered at the UNECE/UN-ESCAP "Forum on Sustainable Transport Connectivity between Europe and Asia" (WP.5/WP.24 session; 17 slides). DATED ~2019/2020 (membership slide "Oct 2019"; incident list to Jan 2019 — status/membership has grown since). GROUNDS THE ER-ISAC WATCH with a primary source (the Chairman's own account) — ER-ISAC = European Rail Information Sharing and Analysis Center, the RAIL SECTOR CYBER threat-intelligence-sharing body: an inclusive non-profit platform for EU railway undertakings to collectively exchange info on + tackle cyber-security threats (two-way public-private sharing). The RAIL EQUIVALENT of the GSMA T-ISAC (E-2026-08-01-36) and the candidate owner for the §4a OPERATIONAL-SECURITY GAP. FOUNDED 4 June 2019; 54 organisations (Oct 2019); Co-Chairs FR/DE/BE/NL; members FI/NO/DK/IT/CH/AT/CZ; member orgs incl. DB, DB Netze, Infrabel, SNCB, SNCF Réseau, SNCF Mobilités, ProRail, NS; Contact@ER-ISAC.EU. KEY CONTENT: THREAT LANDSCAPE (slide 4) — rail tech = signalling + traffic mgmt, MOST are SAFETY-RELATED (interlocking, speed control, traffic mgmt, automatic driving, SCADA, remote monitoring/supervision, GSM-R, ETCS-L2); IMs/RUs use the SAME technologies + methods + SAME SUPPLY CHAIN across countries; "OBSOLESCENCE of safety systems exposed to current + future cyber threats"; "STANDARDS FOR SAFETY IN RAILWAY NOT UP TO DATE WITH CURRENT CYBERSECURITY CHALLENGES"; "One issue affects us All"; DIGITISATION cyber risks (slide 5): datacentres-on-wheels, publicly-accessible customer info, ERTMS safety-control digitisation, OT, IoT, predictive maintenance (RTM); "GREAT NEED FOR POLICY AND OVERSIGHT" (slide 6): rail industry cyber-risk control IMMATURE + threat-sophistication increases surface = more incidents; rail-cyber incident list (Dublin Luas tram website 2019, IRCTC India 2019, Great Western Railway ~1000 accounts 2018, WannaCry/Deutsche Bahn ticketing 2017, four cyber attacks UK railways 2016, Chaos Computer Club 32C3 2015); ISAC ROLE (slide 7, ENISA-sourced): non-profit central threat-info resource + public-private sharing; first EU ISACs = finance/energy. | E-2026-08-02-02 | 2026-08-02 | Singapore IMDA (Infocomm Media Development Authority) advisory — "Compromised GRX Networks Delivers GTPDOOR Linux Malware" ("Advisory for ICM Sectors"; original report published 2024-02-27, updated 2024-03-17; 3 pp). Government-agency cyber advisory RELAYING security researcher "HaxRob"'s GTPDOOR discovery (technical primary = the HaxRob / doubleagent.net writeup, ref [1], NOT on file). GROUNDS THE §4a GTPDOOR EXEMPLAR with a primary government advisory — GTPDOOR = a Linux backdoor targeting systems ADJACENT TO THE GRX (GPRS Roaming eXchange): SGSN / GGSN / P-GW (roaming/interconnect components exposed to public roaming partners, with direct access to the telecom core). ATTRIBUTED to LightBasin (UNC1945) — an intelligence-collection threat group targeting telecoms globally. MO (the §4a thesis made concrete): listens for magic GTP-C packets, blends C2 into legitimate interconnect traffic, can change its process name to mimic legitimate system processes; PRE-REQUISITE = the roaming-partner environment / carrier network must be compromised first. | E-2026-08-02-07 | 2026-08-02 | El-moussa, Mudhar & Jones (BT Centre for Information & Security Research / Security Design and Operate; Jones also Edith Cowan University) — "Overview of SIP Attacks and Countermeasures" (in D. Weerasinghe (Ed.), ISDF 2009 — Int'l Conf. on Information Security and Digital Forensics; Springer LNICST vol. 41, pp. 82–91; © ICST 2010; PEER-REVIEWED conference paper). A SIP threat TAXONOMY + countermeasures — in scope because FRMCS voice = 3GPP MCX (MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo) over IMS, which is SIP-signalled (the GSM-R→SIP modernization thread, E-2026-07-30-14..-22). TAXONOMY (effect-level, no how-to): §2.1 DoS/flooding — SIP REGISTER flooding, INVITE/call flooding; §2.2 injection — buffer overflow (RCE via SIP-stack vuln; Cisco IP-phone exemplar), RTP media injection; §2.3 spoofing (SIP auth is OPTIONAL → requests processed unauthenticated) — DE-REGISTRATION (spoofed REGISTER Expires:0 silences a user), BYE/call-teardown, re-send, CALL HIJACKING (de-reg + re-register as victim → redirect/eavesdrop); §2.4 authentication — DoS-auth (MD5-compute exhaustion), dictionary/enumeration; §2.5 traffic capture/eavesdrop (RTP→WAV); §2.6 clear-text message modification; §2.7 SPIT (voice spam). COUNTERMEASURES: strong authentication, SIP-over-TLS, SRTP for media, ingress filtering, VLAN separation of signalling/media, IDS/IPS with SIP-grammar learning, patching, DEFENCE-IN-DEPTH. VINTAGE CAVEAT (2009/2010): the attack TAXONOMY (the SIP-signalling threat CLASSES) is layer-valid and still applies to any SIP-signalled system incl. MCX/IMS — cite at concept level; but the SPECIFIC crypto/mitigations it names are SUPERSEDED — TLS RFC 4346 = TLS 1.1 (→ TLS 1.3 RFC 8446), MD5 digest auth (deprecated), RFC 2401 IPsec (→ RFC 4301, E-2026-08-02-08) — do NOT cite its mechanisms as current; the modern equivalents live in 3GPP TS 33.180 (MC-service security: SIP-signalling protection, TLS, MIKEY key mgmt, identity mgmt) + TS 33.501/33.210 (transport) + SUBSET-146 (ERA app E2E), all on file. VALIDATES, does not flip: R14/ADR-012 (Open · Proposed) — this is the APPLICATION/SIP-SIGNALLING-LAYER threat taxonomy the FRMCS MCX voice plane inherits; it COMPLEMENTS the existing vector taxonomy (ATT&CK-ICS rail-OT + FiGHT 5G + MoTIF mobile-bearer — all network/radio/interconnect layer) by adding the SIP/app-signalling layer ABOVE the bearer; the de-registration/hijack/BYE-teardown/registrar-flood classes are precisely why MCX mandates SIP integrity+confidentiality + strong auth (33.180) rather than the OPTIONAL MD5 digest this paper laments; R5 (Accepted) — MCX MCData/MCVideo/voice ride SIP/IMS; PR15 (High) — the REC/group-call/functional-addressing features (the GSM-R equivalence bar) are MCPTT-over-SIP, so their signalling security is governed by this class; R12 (Proposed) — the SIP-grammar-learning IDS/IPS + anomaly detection = Risk-Sentinel input at the signalling layer; ADR-007/SIM-SEC — the SIP-signalling class is a candidate adversary-emulation layer. AVAILABILITY ANGLE (light): SIP REGISTER/INVITE flooding + de-registration are DoS-on-the-registrar vectors — an availability-attack complement to the R3/R4 outage thread, though those reqs are outage-driven not attack-driven. DOES NOT flip a status: R14 Open, ADR-012/R12 Proposed, R5 Accepted, PR15 High, ADR-007 unchanged; no matrix/ADR change (taxonomy enrichment — adds the SIP/app-signalling layer to the vector set). SECURITY item → also indexed in evidence-log-security.md; defensive/taxonomy, no single attack vector, effect-level only (no operational how-to). | E-2026-08-02-08 | 2026-08-02 | IETF RFC 4301 "Security Architecture for the Internet Protocol" (Kent & Seo, BBN; December 2005; Standards Track / Proposed Standard) — THE CURRENT IPsec ARCHITECTURE. One-row-per-spec ([[evidence-log-one-row-per-spec]]): this row = the IPsec Security Architecture spec-family, anchored on the CURRENT edition RFC 4301; version history RFC 1825 (1995) → RFC 2401 (1998) → RFC 4301 (2005, current). RFC 4301 OBSOLETES RFC 2401; UPDATED BY RFC 6040 (ECN tunnelling) + RFC 7619 (IKEv2 NULL auth). SPECIFIES: IP-layer security for IPv4/IPv6 — AH (Authentication Header) + ESP (Encapsulating Security Payload); Security Associations (SA; auth/integrity/confidentiality/anti-replay/traffic-flow-confidentiality); SPD (policy) + SAD (active SAs); TRANSPORT vs TUNNEL mode; key mgmt (IKEv2 default). CHANGES vs RFC 2401 (the edition the SIP paper E-2026-08-02-07 [7] cited): + PEER AUTHORIZATION DATABASE (PAD) linking IKE↔SPD (separates peer-auth from traffic-authz); enhanced/decorrelated SPD-SAD model + multiple-SPD; EXTENDED (64-bit) SEQUENCE NUMBERS; multicast SA longest-match SAD lookup; IPv6 extension-header selector handling; ICMP + Mobility-Header selectors; mandates IKEv2-level features. READ: datatracker header + abstract/overview (both editions: rfc4301 current, rfc2401 obsolete) | IETF standards-track RFC (primary — authoritative IETF document; RFC 4301 = CURRENT edition, obsoletes RFC 2401) | https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc4301 (RFC 4301, 2005, current; obsoletes https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc2401 RFC 2401, 1998) | A | R14/ADR-012 (the IPsec transport-security architecture underlying 3GPP NDS/IP) + R5 (secure packet-native transport) | validate | A — PRIMARY IETF RFC, CURRENT edition. RFC 4301 (2005) is the operative IPsec architecture — it OBSOLETES RFC 2401 (1998); cite 4301, NOT 2401. 3GPP TS 33.210 (NDS/IP — mandates IPsec ESP for the network-domain/interconnect transport, E-2026-08-01-03) profiles the RFC 4301 architecture (ESP tunnel-mode between security gateways/SEGs) for the FRMCS 5G + interconnect transport; RFC 4301's PAD/SPD/SAD model is what NDS/IP + the 5G SEG/N32 border enforce. This row supersedes-in-place the earlier RFC-2401 anchor: RFC 2401 is the DATED edition the 2009 SIP paper (E-07) cited — consistent with the E-07 vintage caveat (TLS 1.1 / MD5 / RFC 2401 all superseded; RFC 4301 + TLS 1.3 + 33.180 are the current answers). VALIDATES, does not flip: R14/ADR-012 (Open · Proposed) — grounds the IPsec-architecture layer (AH/ESP/SA/SPD/SAD/PAD/tunnel-mode) beneath the 3GPP NDS/IP stack (33.210 ESP + 33.501 transport) on file; R5 (Accepted) — secure packet-native transport. DOES NOT move a decision: R14 Open, ADR-012 Proposed; no matrix/ADR change (records the current IPsec-architecture edition + supersession). SECURITY item → also indexed in evidence-log-security.md; architecture reference, no attack vector. ONE-ROW-PER-SPEC: IPsec Security Architecture = RFC 1825 → RFC 2401 → RFC 4301 (this) — single row, current-edition anchor; RFC 4301 itself updated by RFC 6040 + RFC 7619 (component updates, not new architecture editions). FOLLOW-UP: none — RFC 4301 is the live anchor; watch for a future architecture-level obsoletion. KIN: E-2026-08-02-07 (SIP paper — cited the obsolete RFC 2401 as [7]; the vintage-caveat thread), E-2026-08-01-03 (3GPP TS 33.210 NDS/IP — mandates IPsec ESP, profiles RFC 4301), E-2026-08-01-02 (SCAS), sdo-map §2 IETF row, ADR-012, R14/R5 |
| E-2026-08-02-09 | 2026-08-02 | UIC FRMCS Functional Interface Specification (FIS), ref FIS-7970 v1.0.0, 13 Feb 2023 (FRMCS FIS Working Group; ISBN 978-2-7461-3127-9; © UIC Paris 2023; 73 pp). THE FRMCS END-TO-END FIS — translates the FRMCS FRS functional requirements into 3GPP MCX end-to-end TRANSACTION FLOWS: "which telecommunication service primitives have to be used and how" by any FRMCS client application. The "E-2-E FIS" layer of the FRMCS spec set (Fig 1: FRS → SRS → E-2-E FIS + FFFIS/OBapp/TSapp); completes the UIC core set on file (FRS FU-7120 E-2026-07-26-13, SRS FW-AT-7800 E-2026-08-01-33, FFFIS FW-AT-7950 E-2026-08-01-21). SCOPE: MCX-service-layer only — EDITOR'S NOTE: IMS/SIPCore service-layer requirements are FFS (for-further-study); the FRMCS service client = a 3GPP MCX client (per ETSI TR 103 459). CONTENT: §1.4 FRMCS services access modes — OBapp/TSapp Tight-Coupled (TC) / Loose-Coupled (LC) / Super-Loose-Coupled (SLC); §1.5 LOCAL BINDING function (OBapp control-plane security: mutual auth + integrity + confidentiality between On-Board App and OB-FRMCS); §2 gateway-access-mode service sessions (user registration, session start/end, incoming-session notification); §3 COMMON FUNCTIONS — role management (user identities: login, ETCS ATP/ATO, REC/VOICE; FUNCTIONAL IDENTITIES = functional alias activation/de-activation/usage = functional addressing), location services, arbitration; §4 APPLICATIONS — ATP (registration, location, QoS/priority, session, ATP-TS responsibility handover, PKI/KMS), ATO (+ PKI security-certificate mgmt), REC (types, generic principles, QoS/priority, generic flows: setup, entry/leaving addressed area, floor request), VOICE, TCMS; §5 voice-app coordinating function; §6 FRMCS/GSM-R INTERWORKING; Annex A REC implementation options (2A/2B/2B-dispatcher-centric/4); Annex B EU interoperability requirements. Normative refs pull the full 3GPP MCX set (TS 22.280/23.280/23.282/24.282/24.379/24.482/24.483/33.180) + ERTMS (ERA_ERTMS_040001, SUBSET-098/037-1/026-7). Completes the UIC FRMCS core spec set on file: FRS (FU-7120, E-2026-07-26-13) → SRS (FW-AT-7800, E-2026-08-01-33) → FIS (this, E-2-E) + FFFIS (FW-AT-7950, E-2026-08-01-21) — the FIS is the layer that TRANSLATES the FRS into concrete MCX transaction flows. VALIDATES, does not flip: R1/R5/R7 (Accepted) — the FIS is a core standards-anchored FRMCS spec; R2 (Accepted) — §6 FRMCS/GSM-R interworking = the coexistence/migration mechanism at the functional-interface layer; PR15 (High) — STRONGEST functional grounding yet of the GSM-R feature-equivalence bar: REC (= GSM-R Railway Emergency Call/Notruf), FUNCTIONAL IDENTITIES / functional alias (= GSM-R functional addressing, E-2026-07-30-25 baseline), VOICE, ARBITRATION, QoS/priority (= eMLPP) are all specified here as concrete MCX end-to-end flows with call-flow diagrams — the equivalence features are not just asserted but SPECIFIED as MCX transactions (FP2-MORANE-2 E-2026-08-02-05/-06 is validating exactly these flows). SECURITY ELEMENTS (R14, secondary): §1.5 LOCAL BINDING (OBapp mutual auth/integrity/confidentiality) + §4.1.7/4.2.7 PKI/KMS sessions + user auth via IdMS/MCX — all delegating to 3GPP TS 33.180 (SRS §15 E-2026-08-01-33 + the security stack); NOT a dedicated security spec (security embedded in the app flows, no dedicated cyber clause like SRS §15) → master log only, cross-ref the security register. IMS/SIPCore-FFS NOTE (kin E-2026-08-02-07 SIP paper): the FIS specifies ONLY MCX-service-layer flows — IMS/SIPCore requirements FFS; the FRMCS functional interface is defined at the MCX (MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo) abstraction ABOVE the raw SIP/IMS layer the E-07 taxonomy addresses — SIP-signalling security therefore sits in 33.180 beneath this MCX-service abstraction. DOES NOT move a decision: R1/R5/R7/R2 Accepted, PR15 High, R14 Open; no matrix/ADR status change (core-spec grounding; strengthens PR15 feasibility on primary evidence, no flip). NOT primarily a security item → master log only. FOLLOW-UP: the FRMCS TOBA FRS (TOBA-7510, N4) if not yet on file (5th UIC core spec); the FIS Compliancy Statement FIS-7971 (I4, informative FFS). VALIDATES, does not flip: R7 (Accepted, ADR-001) — the PRIMARY multi-vendor interoperability proof: 1508 tests / 95.0% success across 15 MCX-server + 12 MCX-client vendors on 3GPP MCX over 4G/5G = FRMCS's interoperability premise demonstrated in independent testing; R1/R5 (Accepted) — FRMCS = 3GPP MCX (the stream-C FRMCS/5G slice); PR15 (High) — MCPTT/MCData/MCVideo + REC-adjacent + TETRA-interworking feature testing across vendors (the feature-equivalence set exercised in interop); R2 (Accepted) — the IWF (Interworking Function) pattern = the legacy-interworking/coexistence mechanism (here TETRA/LMR, not GSM-R, but the same architectural pattern as the FRMCS/GSM-R interworking of FIS §6, E-2026-08-02-09). R8 STRENGTHENED (Recommended — policy-level open, NO flip): the large multi-vendor MCX ecosystem interoperating on standardised 3GPP interfaces is strong TECHNICAL grounding for the unbundling/anti-lock-in thesis (many substitutable vendors, cross-vendor interop proven) — but the PROCUREMENT policy is a separate decision, so R8 stays 'policy-level open'; technical feasibility of unbundling ≠ the policy choice. HONEST MATURITY CAVEAT: the FRMCS/5G stream (C) is THINNER + newer than the mature MCX-over-4G ecosystem (Stream A: 15 servers) — FRMCS-specific interop (C: ~3 servers / ~3 clients) is EMERGING, not yet at the maturity of general MCX-over-LTE; so the R7/R8 grounding is STRONG for MCX generally, EMERGING for the FRMCS-specific slice. CURRENCY: this is the 8TH Plugtests (Oct 2023, Rel-17); later MCX/FRMCS Plugtests + the UIC 2025 Plugtest (E-2026-07-11-01) have occurred SINCE — a point-in-time datapoint, not the current interop state (WATCH for later reports). MOBILE THREAT TAXONOMY (per-domain): Device (SIM manipulation/cloning/firmware hacks/TFTP MitM), Air Interface (MitM/jamming), RAN (rogue nodes/insecure S1-X2-Xn), MEC & Backhaul (DDoS/insecure Sx-N6/CP-UP sniffing/side-channel/NFVi), 5G Packet Core & OAM (virtualization/LI/access-control/network-slice/API-NEF/roaming-partner/DDoS), SGi-N6 & External Roaming (IoT/VAS/app-server/API). Nokia's annual telecom-security threat landscape — sources: Nokia NetGuard + Deepfield telemetry, Managed Security Services ops, Nokia Bell Labs, cybersecurity consulting + quantum-safe networking, + a summer-2025 global survey of telecom security leaders. THESIS: telecoms a KEY TARGET 2024-2025; coordinated infrastructure compromise (not opportunistic) systematically targeting LAWFUL-INTERCEPTION systems, MOBILE-CORE SIGNALING, ORCHESTRATION layers, SUBSCRIBER DATABASES. SECTIONS: telecom-sector attack trends, DDoS trends, zero-day, operational threats/technical shifts, QUANTUM-computing threats, regulatory landscape, key strategic directions of CSPs, Nokia's cybersecurity expertise. HEADLINE STATS (survey): 63% of operators faced ≥1 "living off the land" (LOTL) attack last year (32% saw 4+); 76% of vulns from missing patches; 59% of breaches from human error/insider; 55% report telecom-adapted malware, 45.1% custom toolkits; 63% of major incidents take >1 week to fully recover; DDoS residential-proxy botnets now 100M+ endpoints, terabit-scale (78% of campaigns done within 5 min); cert validity shrinking 398→47 days by 2029 (PQC migration "in its infancy"); "By 2028, over half of telecom operators expect to run HIGHLY OR FULLY AUTONOMOUS SOCs — success depends on how well they are GOVERNED and SECURED to maintain trust at full speed." MAJOR INCIDENTS 2024-2025 (Table 1, OSINT-based, attribution per public reporting): GTPDoor/suspected LightBasin (late-2023–Feb-2024, covert exfil), SALT TYPHOON (Sep-Oct 2024, lawful-interception intrusion), BPFDoor on HSS (Apr 2025, ~26.95M USIM/IMSI records, SIM-cloning risk), fake-base-station SMS fraud (Turkey), submarine-cable faults, UNC1549 (LinkedIn-lure espionage), SIM-server/swatting abuse. SALT TYPHOON deep-dive: APT (active ≥2019); 80+ countries; IOS XE device-level persistence; GRE/IPsec tunnels, firmware/config tampering, credential theft, traffic mirroring, LOTL (PsExec/Impacket); GhostSpider/Masol-RAT/Demodex; joint CSA 27 Aug 2025. TYPHOON COHORT (Microsoft taxonomy): Salt (telecom), Volt (critical infra, LOTL), Flax (IoT/SOHO, Mirai), Charcoal (gov/edu/energy, phishing+LLM-misuse), Salmon (defense/crypto), Silk (IT supply chain/MSPs). BPFDoor+USIM: S.Korea MSIT-confirmed; BPF kernel-level magic-packet backdoor, firewall bypass, process masquerading; ~26.96M IMSI exfil → nationwide SIM-replacement (kin GTPDOOR magic-packet class). | E-2026-08-02-13 | 2026-08-02 | ETSI White Paper No. 71 — "AI-native Infrastructure, next phase of Telco Cloud evolution", 1st edition, May 2026 (ETSI-published; NON-NORMATIVE vision/position paper; authored by a multi-operator/vendor group: Turkcell, Rakuten Mobile, NEC, Türk Telekom, NTT Docomo, SK Telecom, Huawei, ZTE, China Mobile/Telecom/Unicom, Modsoft, BT). POSITIONS the future Telco Cloud as an AI-NATIVE INFRASTRUCTURE — AI models + agents as "first-class citizens" embedded in the cloud-platform core (service enablement, lifecycle mgmt, CLOSED-LOOP operations, resource optimisation), evolving INCREMENTALLY on the existing ETSI NFV + cloud-native base. THESIS: traditional human-driven ops are inadequate for telecom scale/complexity → embed intelligence + autonomy. TRENDS (§2.4): cloud-native AI "from container-centric to AGENTIC-CENTRIC"; OAM "from automation-centric to INTELLIGENT AI". CHALLENGES (§3.4): resource heterogeneity, low-latency/high-reliability in distributed clouds, NEW SECURITY + energy concerns of AI workloads. Calls for ETSI + 3GPP + open-source collaboration. | E-2026-08-02-14 | 2026-08-02 | ETSI White Paper No. 62 — "A Vision for Communications Security" (running title "A Vision for Telecommunications Security"), 1st edition, May 2024 (ETSI-published, developed in ETSI TC CYBER; NON-NORMATIVE vision/position paper). Authors: Charles Brookson (OBE; ex-Chairman ETSI OCG Security / TC CYBER; 25-yr GSMA Security Group chair; GSM/3GPP security veteran), Laurence Wayne (BT Group; TC CYBER rapporteur for the 6G-security groundwork), Scott Cadzow (ETSI Fellow; TC CYBER rapporteur), Faraz Naim (Accenture UK). A VISION to guide future-network (esp. 6G) security standardization + design. CORE CONCEPT: an INTELLIGENT TRUSTED NETWORK (ITN) — an OVERLAY trusted network imposing a ZERO-TRUST regime across a "NETWORK OF NETWORKS" (4G/5G/6G + satellite/LEO + IoT/LoRa/Zigbee + Wi-Fi + private networks), securing endpoints/users WITHOUT relying on other networks' security. KEY THEMES: legacy protocols (TCP/IP + SS7, "over 40 years old", known weaknesses) must be carried yet cannot be wholly relied on → the ITN overlay compensates; ZERO TRUST (13 principles — nothing trusted by default: no entity/device/identity/protocol/third-party/hardware/software; trust re-established every use; detect+indicate trust failure; DoS resilience; dynamically-reconfigurable endpoint connections to continue through disruption; resilient to classical + QUANTUM cryptanalysis w/ quantum-resistant algorithms); MINIMUM BASELINE SECURITY STANDARD (MBSS) + AUTONOMOUS SECURITY ASSURANCE; end-user/device/customer perspective. LEGISLATIVE context cited (UK-centric): UK Telecommunications (Security) Act 2021, PSTI Bill, ENISA 5G Cybersecurity Standards report. Precedent: Integrated Adaptive Cyber Defense (IACD). Transition to 6G framed evolutionary (build on existing) + revolutionary (the ITN); open-hardware/Open-RAN agility; environmental sustainability. READ: cover, authors, ToC, exec summary, context, vision, objectives/Zero-Trust principles (pp 1-8) | ETSI White Paper (A — ETSI-published, developed in TC CYBER by named security veterans (Brookson/Cadzow); NON-NORMATIVE vision/position, NOT a TS/EN deliverable; LESS vendor-conflicted than WP-71 — security-consultant/BT authorship) | file "ETSI-WP-62-Vision_for_Telecommunications.pdf", Download folder = ETSI White Paper No. 62 "A Vision for Communications Security" (1st ed., May 2024), etsi.org | A | R14/ADR-012 (communications-security vision) + §4a operational-security gap (ZERO-TRUST interconnect-trust model — the design answer to the "Network of Networks" trust problem) + R2 (legacy-protocol coexistence — SS7/TCP/IP weakness through the transition) + ADR-002 (Autonomous Security Assurance) + R12 (Risk-Sentinel — trust-failure detection/indication) + R3/R4 (resilience — DoS + reconfigurable-through-disruption) + PQC thread (quantum-resistant crypto) | watch | A — ETSI WHITE PAPER (developed in ETSI TC CYBER by named security veterans — Brookson OBE ex-TC-CYBER-chair, Cadzow ETSI Fellow, BT rapporteur), but NON-NORMATIVE: a vision/position "requirements recipe" (May 2024), NOT a TS/EN deliverable — read as security-design DIRECTION; LESS vendor-conflicted than WP-71 (E-2026-08-02-13) — security-consultant + BT authorship, not a vendor bloc. CORROBORATES, does not flip: R14/ADR-012 (Open · Proposed) — the ETSI TC CYBER security VISION for future networks (6G/Network-of-Networks); §4a OPERATIONAL-SECURITY GAP — the ZERO-TRUST / INTELLIGENT-TRUSTED-NETWORK (ITN) overlay is the DESIGN-PRINCIPLE ANSWER to the interconnect-trust problem the §4a gap identifies: "no other network trusted by default", "no protocol trusted by default", "no third party trusted by default", trust re-established every use — directly counters the "Wild West" interconnect trust model (ENISA E-2026-08-01-05) that GTPDOOR/SS7 exploitation (E-2026-08-02-02, SIM-SEC-004) rides; the ITN "Network of Networks" Zero-Trust overlay is the architectural response; R2 (Accepted) — explicitly names the legacy SS7/TCP/IP (>40-yr, known weaknesses) coexistence-security problem through the transition (the dual-run weakest link); ADR-002 (Proposed) — "Autonomous Security Assurance" + MBSS = the autonomous-security-oversight dimension (kin the agentic-oversight thread; Nokia "autonomous SOCs" E-2026-08-02-12, GSMA §6 E-2026-08-01-36); R12 (Proposed) — Zero-Trust principle 9 "detect + indicate where trust has failed" + DoS-consideration = Risk-Sentinel design input; R3/R4 (Open) — principle 11 (DoS resilience) + principle 12 (dynamically-reconfigurable endpoint connections to continue through disruption) = the fail-soft/resilience objective; PQC thread — principle 13 (resilient to classical + QUANTUM cryptanalysis, quantum-resistant algorithms) refreshes the Mosca/PQC thread (GSMA §7 E-2026-08-01-36, Nokia PQC E-2026-08-02-12). HONEST CAVEATS: (1) NON-NORMATIVE vision (a "requirements recipe", prioritisation "to be discussed further") — direction, not a spec; (2) 6G-FORWARD-LOOKING (May 2024; "5G until 2025", 6G planning phase) — the ITN is a future-network concept, not current FRMCS (5G-SA); (3) LEGISLATIVE refs are UK-CENTRIC (TSA 2021, PSTI) — the engagement's regime is EU/DE (CRA/NIS-2, E-2026-07-01-06); cite the security-DESIGN vision (Zero Trust, ITN, MBSS, PQC), NOT the UK legislative mapping as the engagement's regime. DOES NOT move a decision: R14 Open, ADR-012/R12/ADR-002 Proposed, R2 Accepted, R3/R4 Open; no matrix/ADR status change (ETSI security-vision corroboration of the Zero-Trust interconnect + PQC + autonomous-assurance threads). SECURITY item → also indexed in evidence-log-security.md; security-vision/design, no attack vector. FOLLOW-UP: the ETSI TC CYBER 6G-security normative work this WP lays groundwork for (vision → spec pipeline); the ENISA 5G Cybersecurity Standards report it cites; map Zero-Trust/ITN principles to the FRMCS security stack (33.501/33.180/SUBSET-146) as a gap-check. ## How to append

When something arrives during the day:
1. Add a row with the next `E-` id.
2. Set the trust tier honestly (A–D). Discount vendor superlatives; mark opinion as opinion.
3. Note which requirement(s) it touches and the action.
4. If it changes a decision or status, also edit traceability-matrix.md and the relevant ADR, then note "revise" here.
5. At end of day, run `scripts/baseline.sh` to freeze the state.

- **GSMA FS.11 (SS7) / FS.19 (Diameter) / FS.20 (GTP) interconnect-security guidelines — MEMBER-ONLY, accepted residual (recorded 2026-08-01).** Unlike the purchasable CENELEC/IEC texts, these are membership-gated. **Narrowed by the public set on file** (FS.57 MoTIF E-2026-08-01-04 · FS.61 micro-segmentation -07 · FS.56 MDSCert -08 · FS.40 5G Security Guide -09): GSMA's architecture guidance, assurance schemes (NESAS/SCAS, FS.40 §18), legacy-coexistence risks (FS.40 §12) and interconnect border functions (IPUPS, FS.40 §11) are all publicly documented — what stays gated is the detailed operational hardening parameters. **The gating is itself evidence for the §4a gap:** the operational interconnect-security knowledge for the technology FRMCS adopts lives inside a membership club rail doesn't belong to — the ETSI-TC-RT-for-GSMA substitution transfers the architecture role, not the membership benefits. **Verify-watch:** is DB InfraGO (or any FRMCS operator / ERA / UIC) a GSMA member or T-ISAC participant? National-roaming MNO partners (INB 2026, E-2026-07-28-01) have full access — a possible contractual channel. Grounding fallback: ENISA 2018 (E-2026-08-01-05, produced with GSMA input) + public technical analyses citing FS.20 (GTPDOOR write-ups).
